377 — Differential Privacy Engine from Scratch

Project Number: 377

Project Name: Differential Privacy Engine from Scratch

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

1.1 Precise Engineering Definition

Build a production-quality differential privacy engine in Rust that provides:

  • Bounded-sensitivity query execution with rigorous global sensitivity computation and enforcement.
  • Calibrated noise mechanisms (Laplace, Gaussian/analytic-Gaussian, exponential, report-noisy-max, sparse vector technique) backed by secure CSPRNG samplers that resist floating-point representable-value attacks (Mironov 2012).
  • Composable privacy accounting (basic, advanced, Renyi-DP, zero-concentrated-DP, moments accountant) under a hard per-dataset privacy budget enforced at the type-system and runtime level.
  • Local-DP primitives (randomized response, frequency estimation) and DP-SGD (per-example gradient clipping + Gaussian noise + moments-accountant tracking).
  • Statistical conformance validation (neighboring-dataset distribution tests, sensitivity audits) that verifies each mechanism meets its claimed (ε, δ)-DP guarantee.

1.2 System Boundary

In scope Out of scope
All 12 Rust library crates (dp-core through dp-validate) GUI / notebook integration
dp-cli command-line interface Network transport, gRPC/HTTP APIs
Floating-point–safe secure samplers (snapping, discrete Gaussian) Federated aggregation protocol (→ portfolio #407)
RDP/zCDP/moments accountant composition MPC protocols (→ portfolio #392)
DP-SGD training loop integration point Full ML framework / tensor math
Per-dataset + per-analyst budget ledger Multi-party trust negotiation
Statistical conformance tests (StatDP-style) Formal verification / proof assistants
Pure DP and (ε,δ)-DP; add/remove neighboring relation Replace-one neighboring relation (deferred)

1.3 Non-Goals

  • No HTTP/RPC server; the engine is a library + CLI.
  • No persistent database of datasets; the engine operates on in-process slices/iterators.
  • No distributed execution; concurrency is intra-process only.
  • No GPU noise generation; CPU CSPRNG is sufficient and auditable.
  • No zero-knowledge proofs or cryptographic DP attestations.
  • Formal verification of mechanism proofs → PhaseDeferred("Phase 14").
  • Replace-one sensitivity and local sensitivity → PhaseDeferred("Phase 14").
  • Shuffle model of local DP → PhaseDeferred("Phase 13").

1.4 Intended Deployment Model

Role Usage pattern
Data scientist / analyst Link dp-query + dp-budget; issue DP queries against local dataset slices
ML engineer Link dp-dpsgd; train neural net with DP-SGD via moments accountant
Privacy engineer / auditor Run dp-validate conformance suite; inspect budget ledger audit log
Platform operator Embed dp-budget as a budget-enforcement service within a data-access layer
CLI user dp-cli query / budget / mechanism / validate subcommands

1.5 Phase 0 Deliverables — Locked Crates

Twelve crates delivered across 14 phases:

  1. dp-core — shared types, newtypes, error types; no I/O.
  2. dp-sensitivity — L1/L2 global sensitivity analysis; clamping; transform stability.
  3. dp-sample — snapping-mechanism Laplace; discrete/analytic Gaussian; CSPRNG.
  4. dp-mechanism — Laplace, Gaussian, exponential, report-noisy-max mechanisms.
  5. dp-svt — sparse vector technique; above-threshold.
  6. dp-accountant — basic/advanced composition; RDP; zCDP; moments accountant.
  7. dp-budget — per-dataset/per-analyst budget ledger; atomic spend; hard ceiling.
  8. dp-query — count, bounded sum, mean, histogram, quantile; sensitivity wiring.
  9. dp-local — randomized response; frequency estimation; debiasing.
  10. dp-dpsgd — per-example gradient clipping; Gaussian noise; moments-accountant tracking.
  11. dp-validate — statistical DP conformance tests; sensitivity audits.
  12. dp-cli — query | budget | mechanism | validate subcommands.

1.6 What Later Phases Unlock

Phase range Capability unlocked
Phase 1–2 Type-safe privacy parameters; bounded sensitivity
Phase 3–5 Pure ε-DP Laplace, (ε,δ)-DP Gaussian, secure float-safe samplers
Phase 6–7 Exponential mechanism, report-noisy-max, randomized response
Phase 8–10 Full accounting stack: basic/advanced composition, RDP, zCDP, moments
Phase 11 Hard budget enforcement: per-dataset and per-analyst ledgers
Phase 12 Query-level API: count/sum/mean/histogram/quantile
Phase 13 Local DP, DP-SGD training integration, CLI
Phase 14 Statistical conformance, fuzz hardening, performance, packing

1.7 Deferred Features

  • Replace-one neighboring sensitivity → PhaseDeferred("Phase 14").
  • Shuffle-model local DP amplification → PhaseDeferred("Phase 13").
  • Formal proofs / proof-carrying code → PhaseDeferred("Phase 14").
  • Distributed budget coordination (multi-node) → PhaseDeferred("Phase 11").

1.8 Timeline Estimates

Milestone Wall-clock
Phase 1–2 (core + sensitivity) Week 1–2
Phase 3–5 (mechanisms + secure samplers) Week 3–6
Phase 6–8 (exponential, RNM, basic composition) Week 7–9
Phase 9–11 (RDP/zCDP/moments + budget) Week 10–14
Phase 12–13 (queries + DP-SGD + CLI) Week 15–20
Phase 14 (hardening + conformance + perf) Week 21–26
Full system integration + release ~6 months total

1.9 Language Rationale — Rust (Mandatory)

A DP engine's correctness is a privacy guarantee, and the subtle failure modes are numerical: naive floating-point noise sampling leaks the exact dataset (Mironov 2012), an under-counted sensitivity silently voids the guarantee, and sloppy composition over-spends the budget. Rust gives strong newtypes for Epsilon/Delta/Sensitivity that the type system enforces (so a budget can never be mixed up with a noise scale), control over the CSPRNG and bit-level float handling needed for secure/snapping/discrete samplers, exhaustive enums for the mechanism and accountant taxonomy, and no GC timing variation on secret-touching paths. The mechanisms, samplers, accountants, budget manager, and query layer are all Rust.

1.10 Required Prerequisites

Domain Specific knowledge
Probability theory Laplace/Gaussian distributions; log-sum-exp numerical stability; CDF inversion
DP theory Dwork-Roth monograph through §3; composition theorems; neighboring-dataset definitions
Renyi divergence Orders α, monotonicity, conversion to (ε,δ); RDP composition
Float arithmetic IEEE 754 representable values; subnormals; the Mironov CCS 2012 attack vector
CSPRNG / secure sampling OS entropy sources; rejection sampling; snapping / discrete-Gaussian algorithms
Rust Newtypes; trait objects; rand / rand_core traits; num-traits
Statistical testing Hypothesis testing; empirical CDF distance; neighboring-dataset test construction
Gradient-based ML Autograd; per-example gradient computation; clipping norms

2. Why Build This

2.1 Unique Systems Knowledge

Knowledge domain Depth acquired
DP theory end-to-end From ε-DP definition through moments accountant — rare combination
Secure numerical sampling Snapping mechanism; discrete Gaussian; float-attack resistance
Composable privacy accounting RDP, zCDP, and moments accountant with provable composition
Budget lifecycle management Atomic spend, fail-closed enforcement, audit logging
Statistical conformance testing Constructing neighboring-dataset distribution tests empirically
DP-SGD internals Per-example gradient clipping + noise + accountant interaction
Strong-type privacy engineering Newtypes preventing accidental eps/delta/sensitivity confusion

2.2 Historical and Architectural Significance

  • Dwork et al. 2006 (TCC): Calibrating Noise to Sensitivity establishes DP as a rigorous mathematical contract replacing informal anonymization; this engine implements that contract from scratch.
  • Mironov CCS 2012: The floating-point attack broke every prior practical DP implementation; the snapping mechanism is the countermeasure — implementing it from scratch is rare engineering.
  • CCS 2016 (Abadi et al.): DP-SGD + moments accountant enabled private deep learning; building the accountant from definition is instructive for ML-systems engineers.
  • OpenDP lineage: This engine occupies the same architectural niche as opendp/opendp and Google's differential-privacy library; building it from scratch teaches what those abstractions hide.
  • Privacy-preserving computation is a regulatory and infrastructure requirement (GDPR, CCPA, HIPAA statistical disclosure limitation); engineers who understand the internals can audit third-party claims.

2.3 Engineering Muscles Developed

Muscle How exercised
Numerical robustness Float-safe noise sampling; log-sum-exp in exponential mechanism
Type-driven design Newtype invariants enforced at compile time across 12 crates
Correctness-first TDD Property tests for invariants; statistical conformance for DP guarantees
Composable abstractions Mechanism and Query traits that compose without exposing internals
Security-minded implementation CSPRNG discipline; fail-closed error handling; no information leak in errors
Budget resource management Atomic ledger operations; monotonicity invariants; audit logging
Cross-crate architecture Dependency DAG with no cycles; shared types in dp-core

2.4 Production Engineering Concepts Exercised

  • Hard resource ceilings: Budget manager mirrors rate limiters and memory ceilings in systems software.
  • Atomic operations and idempotency: Budget spend must be atomic; partial accounting is a privacy violation.
  • Fail-closed error handling: Unbounded sensitivity, invalid params, or exhausted budget return errors, never defaults.
  • Audit logging without leakage: Log budget charges, mechanism types, and accountant state; never log raw data or un-noised answers (P10).
  • Parameterized correctness tests: Each mechanism has a property-based test suite; each accountant has a composition cross-check.
  • Layered abstraction with trust boundaries: dp-sample is trusted to produce correct noise; dp-mechanism trusts dp-sample but is independently tested; dp-budget trusts dp-accountant's reported cost.

2.5 Cross-Portfolio Links

Project Relationship
#407 Federated Learning + Secure Aggregation Consumes dp-dpsgd / dp-accountant for privacy-preserving gradient aggregation
#392 Multi-Party Computation Overlaps secure sampling; MPC can replace trusted aggregator
#120 RLHF / Training Pipelines DP-SGD integrates into the RLHF training loop
#112–120 ML Stack Any supervised learning pipeline can adopt dp-dpsgd for DP training
#15 Synapse Data warehouse query layer can integrate dp-query + dp-budget as a DP API

2.6 Calibration Value

  • Implementing the moments accountant from scratch is the fastest way to understand why DP-SGD costs what it costs.
  • Building the snapping mechanism forces understanding of IEEE 754 at a level most engineers never reach.
  • The budget manager teaches fail-closed resource management applicable to any quota system.
  • Statistical DP testing (StatDP-style) builds the skill of empirical verification of probabilistic guarantees.

2.7 What Mastery Looks Like

After building this engine you can:

  • Derive and implement the Laplace and Gaussian mechanisms from the (ε,δ) definition, calibrating noise to L1/L2 sensitivity — Phase 2, Phase 3, Phase 4.
  • Implement float-attack-resistant sampling (snapping mechanism, discrete Gaussian) and explain precisely why naive inverse-CDF sampling leaks the input — Phase 5.
  • Build composable privacy accounting (RDP, zCDP, moments accountant) and compose a tight (ε,δ) across thousands of DP-SGD steps — Phase 8, Phase 9, Phase 10.
  • Implement the exponential mechanism with log-sum-exp numerical stability, plus report-noisy-max and randomized response — Phase 6, Phase 7.
  • Engineer a fail-closed budget manager with atomic spend, a monotone ledger, and tamper-evident audit logging — Phase 11.
  • Wire DP-SGD end to end: per-example gradient clipping + noise + accountant interaction — Phase 13.
  • Empirically validate DP guarantees with neighboring-dataset hypothesis tests (StatDP-style KS / χ² tests) — Phase 14.
  • Design strong-typed privacy APIs (newtypes for ε, δ, sensitivity) that turn parameter confusion into a compile error — Phase 1, Phase 12.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

                      DIFFERENTIAL PRIVACY ENGINE  (bound -> calibrate -> account)
+------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|   dp-cli (query / budget / mechanism / validate)                                    |
|        |                                                                            |
|        v                                                                            |
|   dp-query (count / bounded sum / mean / histogram / quantile)                       |
|        |                          |                                                 |
|        v                          v                                                 |
|   dp-sensitivity (clamp to Bounds; L1/L2 global sensitivity; transform stability)    |
|        |                                                                            |
|        v                                                                            |
|   dp-mechanism (Laplace / Gaussian / exponential / report-noisy-max)  <-- dp-svt     |
|        |   noise calibrated to sensitivity & epsilon                                |
|        v                                                                            |
|   dp-sample (SECURE sampler: snapping Laplace / discrete Gaussian; CSPRNG)           |
|        |                                                                            |
|        v   every release charges the budget                                         |
|   dp-accountant (basic/advanced composition; Renyi-DP; zCDP; moments) ----+          |
|        |                                                                  |          |
|        v                                                                  v          |
|   dp-budget (hard ceiling: DENY if release would exceed remaining budget)            |
|        |                                                                            |
|        +--> dp-local (randomized response, freq estimation)                          |
|        +--> dp-dpsgd (clip per-example grads + Gaussian noise + moments)             |
|        +--> dp-validate (statistical neighboring-dataset tests; sensitivity audit)   |
|        v                                                                            |
|   DpResult<T> { value, (eps,delta) }  (post-processing-immune released answer)        |
+------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

3.2 Subsystem Descriptions

dp-cli — Entry point for human operators. Parses query | budget | mechanism | validate subcommands and dispatches to the corresponding library crate. Owns no business logic; translates CLI arguments to typed PrivacyParams and Bounds. Stdout: DpResult JSON or budget summary. Stderr: errors. No access to raw data in production mode.

dp-query — The analyst-facing query API. Exposes count, bounded_sum, mean, histogram, and quantile operations. Each query type implements the Query trait: computes its own Sensitivity from supplied Bounds, selects the appropriate mechanism via dp-mechanism, and requests a budget spend via dp-budget. Inbound: raw data slice + Bounds + PrivacyParams. Outbound: DpResult<T>. Does not own noise generation; delegates entirely to dp-mechanism.

dp-sensitivity — Computes and enforces global L1/L2 sensitivity. Input: a query description and Bounds. Output: a Sensitivity value certified to be >= the true global sensitivity (I2). Provides clamp(value, bounds) to enforce I6. Provides transform_stability(scale) for composing sensitivities across data transforms. No randomness; deterministic; no I/O.

dp-mechanism — Implements the Mechanism trait for Laplace, Gaussian, exponential, and report-noisy-max. Each mechanism takes (exact: T, sens: Sensitivity, params: PrivacyParams) and returns a noisy T. Noise generation is delegated to dp-sample; the mechanism layer only calibrates parameters (scale b = l1/ε for Laplace; σ from analytic-Gaussian formula for Gaussian) and calls the sampler. Advertises its own PrivacyParams to the accountant. dp-svt is a peer crate that implements the sparse vector technique on top of dp-mechanism primitives.

dp-svt — Sparse vector technique and above-threshold. Accepts a stream of queries and a threshold; answers "above" or "below" while paying ε once per query that crosses the threshold, not once per query answered. Internally uses dp-mechanism's Laplace noise. Useful for answering many queries with a single budget allocation.

dp-sample — Trusted noise source. Implements the Mironov snapping mechanism for Laplace (closes the float-DP attack) and a discrete / interval-refinement Gaussian. Uses a CSPRNG (OS entropy via getrandom). No higher-level logic; only: uniform bit generation → Laplace/Gaussian samples with no representable-value gaps. This is the trust anchor for I5; all other noise passes through here.

dp-accountant — Tracks cumulative privacy loss across releases. Four accountant modes: basic composition (εs add, δs add), advanced composition (sqrt-scaling), RDP (Renyi divergence at a vector of orders α), and zCDP/moments accountant (rho). Provides compose(mechanism_params) -> PrivacyLoss and to_eps_delta(delta_target) -> PrivacyParams. RDP is the default for Gaussian and DP-SGD; converts to tightest (ε,δ) at report time (P6). Monotonic: I9 enforced by construction.

dp-budget — Hard budget enforcer. Holds a Budget { total: PrivacyParams, spent: PrivacyLoss } per dataset/per-analyst scope. Before any release: query accountant for the cost, compare against total - spent; if it would exceed, return BudgetExhausted error (I3, I10). If allowed, atomically update spent. Maintains an append-only audit log of every charge (P10). No access to raw data; operates solely on PrivacyLoss accounting values.

dp-local — Local-DP layer. Implements randomized response with flip probability p for a binary response, and a debiased frequency estimator. Provides heavy-hitter sketches via the RAPPOR-style approach. Does not route through dp-budget (local DP's trust model is user-side); has its own PrivacyParams for the reported ε guarantee. Outbound: DpResult<T> stamped with local-DP ε.

dp-dpsgd — DP-SGD integration. Input: per-example gradient tensors (as f64 slices), a clipping norm bound, and PrivacyParams. Clips each per-example gradient to L2 norm ≤ C, sums, adds Gaussian noise calibrated to (C, ε, δ), and charges the moments accountant once per training step. Returns the noisy batch gradient + updated PrivacyLoss for the training run. Does not implement backpropagation; expects caller-supplied per-example gradients.

dp-validate — Statistical DP conformance testing. Constructs pairs of neighboring datasets; runs a mechanism many times on each; applies a hypothesis test (log-likelihood ratio or empirical CDF test) to verify the ε-bound holds. Also runs sensitivity audits (verify declared sensitivity >= empirical sensitivity). Outputs a conformance report: PASS / FAIL per mechanism + accountant cross-checks.

DpResult<T> — The post-processing-immune output type. Carries { value: T, params: PrivacyParams }. Once emitted, any deterministic or randomized function of value remains DP at the same params (I7). The params field is the provenance record for the released value.

4. System Constraints

4.1 Performance and Latency Targets

Operation Target Notes
Single Laplace/Gaussian noise draw < 1 µs p99 Snapping mechanism overhead over naive rng
Single dp-query (count/sum) end-to-end < 10 µs p99 Includes sensitivity + noise + accountant charge
Histogram query (1 000 bins) < 1 ms p99 Parallel composition; 1000 noise draws
Budget spend (atomic ledger update) < 5 µs p99 Single atomic compare-and-swap sequence
Accountant RDP compose (50 orders) < 20 µs p99 Vec<f64> dot product
dp-validate conformance test (10 000 samples) < 10 s per mechanism Batch; not on critical path
dp-dpsgd per-step overhead (clip + noise) < 100 µs per step for batch ≤ 256 Excluding backprop
CLI startup + first query < 100 ms Process startup dominates

4.2 Memory Ceilings

Component Ceiling Notes
dp-core / dp-sensitivity (in-process) < 1 MB heap Stateless; no accumulation
dp-accountant (RDP curve, 100 orders) < 100 KB per accountant Vec<f64> × 100 × compositions
dp-budget (per-dataset ledger + audit log) < 10 MB per dataset Audit log append-only; 1 M entries × ~64 B
dp-dpsgd gradient buffer (batch 256, 1 M params) < 512 MB Caller-allocated; engine adds one noise buffer
dp-validate sample buffer < 50 MB per test 10 000 samples × 5 KB max output
dp-cli process RSS < 32 MB No persistent data; transient query execution

4.3 Concurrency Constraints

Constraint Value Rationale
Budget ledger concurrency Lock-free CAS or mutex-per-dataset Atomic spend; no TOCTOU between check and charge
Mechanism invocations Embarrassingly parallel across queries Each is stateless given a CSPRNG per thread
CSPRNG threading model One CSPRNG state per thread (thread-local) Avoid contention; rand::thread_rng() pattern
dp-accountant state Single-writer, multiple-reader Composition is append; reads for conversion are concurrent
dp-validate Parallelizable across mechanisms Independent test suites; rayon parallel iterator
Max parallel query threads 64 (soft) OS thread overhead dominates above this for I/O-free workloads

4.4 Correctness and Safety Constraints

Constraint Enforcement
Epsilon > 0 and finite Newtype constructor rejects at construction; I8
Delta in [0, 1) Newtype constructor; Gaussian requires delta > 0
Sensitivity >= 0, l1 >= 0, l2 >= 0 Newtype invariants; panic-free: return Err
Budget spend monotone Ledger spent field only increases; no subtraction
Accountant composition monotone compose only appends; I9
No release on budget exhaustion BudgetExhausted error; no default-zero release; I10
Clamping before aggregation dp-sensitivity enforces I6; out-of-range values clamped
No raw data in logs dp-budget and dp-accountant log only PrivacyLoss values; P10

4.5 Numerical Precision Constraints

Constraint Requirement
Float type f64 throughout; no f32 on privacy-critical paths
Snapping mechanism precision Clamp-then-round to nearest representable; see Mironov 2012 §3
Exponential mechanism Log-sum-exp for numerical stability; never raw exp() overflow
Analytic Gaussian sigma Balle-Wang bisection converges to < 1e-10 relative error
RDP order vector Orders in [1.01, 256]; at least 20 log-spaced values recommended
Privacy-loss arithmetic Use checked_add / saturating semantics; never silent overflow

4.6 Storage and Audit Constraints

Aspect Constraint
Audit log format Append-only structured records: {timestamp, dataset_id, mechanism, eps, delta, remaining}
Audit log persistence Optional; in-process Vec by default; file backend via trait object
Audit log size budget 1 M entries per dataset before rotation required
State serialization PrivacyLoss / Budget serializable to JSON or CBOR for checkpointing
No dataset persistence The engine never writes raw data; only budget and accounting state

4.7 Availability and Failure Budget

Scenario Behavior
Invalid params at construction Immediate Err(InvalidParam); no panic
Budget exhausted Err(BudgetExhausted); caller decides policy (reject, prompt)
CSPRNG entropy failure Propagate OS error; no fallback to weak RNG
Sensitivity overflow (unbounded domain) Err(SensitivityUnbounded); I10
Accountant numeric underflow/overflow Return Err(AccountingOverflow); no silent clamp
Panic policy No panics in library code; all errors are Result<T, DpError>

4.8 Deployment Assumptions

Assumption Detail
Hardware x86-64 or ARM64; no SIMD requirement (no SIMD in critical path)
OS entropy /dev/urandom or getrandom(2) syscall available
Rust toolchain Stable Rust ≥ 1.75; no nightly features
No network I/O in library crates dp-core through dp-validate are pure compute
Embedding model Static or dynamic library; or CLI binary
Dataset size In-process slice; max ~10 GB resident; no streaming requirement in Phase 1–13
Scaling unit Single process; horizontal scaling at the application layer, not engine layer

5. Required Reading

Books

# Source Key Chapters / Sections Concepts to Extract Relevance to This Engine
B1 Dwork & Roth — The Algorithmic Foundations of Differential Privacy (NOW Publishers, 2014) Ch 1–3: definitions, neighboring relation, Laplace/Gaussian/exponential mechanisms, composition; Ch 4: advanced composition; Ch 5: randomized response; Ch 6: SVT / above-threshold; Ch 10: pan-privacy; Appendix A: probability background Pure-DP def, approximate-DP def, add/remove vs replace neighboring; global L1/L2 sensitivity; Laplace calibration b=Δf/ε; Gaussian calibration; post-processing lemma; basic + advanced composition; SVT privacy analysis; exponential mechanism eps-DP proof Every invariant and type in the engine traces to a theorem or definition here. Read end-to-end before writing any crate. Treat as the normative spec; all other references extend or sharpen it.
B2 Vadhan — The Complexity of Differential Privacy (survey, 2017; available on author's site) Sections 2–4: lower bounds and packing; Section 5: composition tightness; Section 6: exponential mechanism lower bounds Computational lower bounds on accuracy vs privacy; packing arguments showing that composition tightness matters; impossibility of simultaneously tight composition and low sample complexity Calibrates reachable utility given a budget; Phase 12 quantile and mean accuracy targets should be checked against these bounds to avoid over-promising
B3 Wood et al. — Differential Privacy: A Primer for a Non-Technical Audience (Vanderbilt Journal, 2018) Section 3: neighboring definitions in practice; Section 4: sensitivity for different query types; Section 5: budget and composition examples Add/remove vs replace neighboring: add/remove doubles sensitivity for count/sum vs replace; sensitivity of mean = (upper−lower)/n under add/remove dp-sensitivity must document which neighboring relation is assumed (add/remove); this choice is a parameter of Bounds and must be explicit
B4 Kairouz et al. — Advances and Open Problems in Federated Learning (2021, arXiv 1912.04977) Section 2.2: local vs central DP; Section 4.3: DP-SGD and its variants; Section 4.6: secure aggregation combined with DP Distinction between local DP (noise at source) and central DP (noise at aggregator); DP-SGD privacy amplification by subsampling; interaction with secure aggregation (portfolio #407) dp-local and dp-dpsgd must each document the DP model (local vs central). Subsampling amplification in dp-dpsgd must use the moments accountant, not informal arguments
B5 Bun & Steinke — Concentrated Differential Privacy: Simplifications, Extensions, and Lower Bounds (TCC 2016; full version arXiv 1605.02065) Sections 1–3: zCDP definition and basic properties; Section 4: Gaussian mechanism satisfies rho-zCDP with rho=l2²/(2σ²); Section 5: composition; Section 6: (ε,δ) conversion formula zCDP definition; additive composition of rho values; conversion: ε = rho + 2sqrt(rho · ln(1/δ)); tighter than advanced composition for repeated Gaussian Foundation for ZcdpAccountant. Derive the conversion formula from Section 6 exactly — an off-by-one in the sqrt term is a real bug
B6 Mironov — Renyi Differential Privacy (CSF 2017; full manuscript arXiv 1702.07476) Sections 2–4: Renyi divergence, RDP definition, basic properties; Section 5: RDP for Laplace, Gaussian, randomized response; Section 6: RDP composition is additive per order; Section 7: RDP → (ε,δ) conversion Alpha-Renyi divergence; M satisfies (α,ε)-RDP means D_α(M(D)
B7 Gaboardi et al. — Programming Language Techniques for Differential Privacy (SIGLOG News, 2016) Sections 2–3: metric-based sensitivity types; Section 4: DFuzz type system; Section 5: implementation strategies Sensitivity as a metric-space property; type-level enforcement of distance tracking; compile-time vs run-time sensitivity verification Motivates P8 (strong types). The dp-core newtype hierarchy directly mirrors the type-theoretic framework — Epsilon, Delta, Sensitivity as distinct types rather than f64 aliases
B8 Erlingsson et al. — Amplification by Shuffling: From Local to Central DP via Anonymity (SODA 2019; full version arXiv 1811.12594) Section 3: shuffling model definition; Section 5: amplification theorem eps_c ≤ O(eps_l · sqrt(ln(1/δ)/n)); Section 6: tightness Privacy amplification when local-DP reports are shuffled before aggregation; the shuffler model as an intermediate between local and central DP dp-local's debiased frequency estimator operates in the central model; if a shuffler is added, amplification can be claimed via this theorem. Note: shuffler not in scope for Phase 13 but the architecture must not preclude it

Papers

# Citation Core Contribution Implementation Relevance Key Theorem / Formula
P1 Dwork, McSherry, Nissim, Smith — "Calibrating Noise to Sensitivity in Private Data Analysis" (TCC 2006) Founding paper: DP definition, Laplace mechanism, global sensitivity, post-processing Phase 3 (LaplaceMechanism), Phase 2 (dp-sensitivity); I1, I2; calibration formula b=Δf/ε Laplace mechanism: add Lap(Δf/ε); global sensitivity Δf = max_{D,D' neighbors}
P2 McSherry & Talwar — "Mechanism Design via Differential Privacy" (FOCS 2007) Exponential mechanism: Pr[output=c] ∝ exp(ε·u(c)/(2Δu)); eps-DP proof; application to selection queries Phase 6 (ExponentialMechanism); utility function sensitivity Δu, not data sensitivity; numerically stable via log-sum-exp Proof: ratio of probabilities for neighbors bounded by exp(ε) because
P3 Mironov — "On Significance of the Least Significant Bits for Differential Privacy" (CCS 2012) IEEE 754 Laplace sampling leaks exact input; snapping mechanism: clamp → snap to λ-grid → add noise on grid Phase 5 (dp-sample); MANDATORY before writing any float sampler; I5; P3 Snapping lambda = 2^k where 2^k ≥ B·eps and B is the output clamp range; the snap-to-grid step makes adjacent inputs indistinguishable
P4 Balle & Wang — "Improving the Gaussian Mechanism for Differential Privacy" (ICML 2018) Analytic calibration: solve Φ(Δ/(2σ)−ε·σ/Δ) − e^ε·Φ(−Δ/(2σ)−ε·σ/Δ) = δ for σ; tighter than classical σ = Δ·sqrt(2·ln(1.25/δ))/ε Phase 4 (GaussianMechanism); use Balle-Wang sigma, not classical; classical is 40% looser in typical regimes Classical: σ_c = Δ·sqrt(2ln(1.25/δ))/ε; analytic: σ_a ≤ σ_c; implement the bisection on the Φ-based equation
P5 Canonne, Kamath, Steinke — "The Discrete Gaussian for Differential Privacy" (NeurIPS 2020) Discrete Gaussian DG(σ²) over integers; sampling algorithm via rejection sampling from a wider discrete distribution; (ε,δ)-DP analysis Phase 5 dp-sample discrete Gaussian sampler; integer-domain queries avoid float-DP attack entirely; sampler algorithm in Section 4 Pr[DG(σ²)=k] ∝ exp(−k²/(2σ²)); rejection sampling: draw from geometric distribution, accept with probability matching the Gaussian ratio
P6 Abadi et al. — "Deep Learning with Differential Privacy" (CCS 2016) DP-SGD: per-example gradient clipping to bound L2 norm C; add Gaussian noise N(0, σ²C²I) to batch gradient; moments accountant for tight ε over T steps Phase 13 (dp-dpsgd); per-example clipping threshold C; noise multiplier σ; moments accountant over steps T and subsampling ratio q=B/N Privacy per step via Poisson subsampling RDP; compose T steps by adding RDP losses; convert to (ε,δ) at end
P7 Dwork, Rothblum, Vadhan — "Boosting and Differential Privacy" (FOCS 2010) Advanced composition theorem: k-fold ε-DP → (ε',δ')-DP with ε'=sqrt(2k·ln(1/δ'))·ε + k·ε(e^ε−1); tighter than k·ε Phase 8 (dp-accountant); the sqrt-scaling formula; when to prefer advanced composition over basic For k queries with ε ≤ 1: ε' ≈ sqrt(2k·ln(1/δ'))·ε; use when k > 2/ε
P8 Ding, Wang, Wang, Zhang — "Detecting Violations of Differential Privacy" (StatDP, CCS 2018) Automated hypothesis testing: generate neighboring dataset pairs, observe output distributions, apply Neyman-Pearson test with p-value threshold related to (ε,δ) Phase 14 (dp-validate); statistical neighboring-dataset test; how to construct the null hypothesis H0: mechanism is (ε,δ)-DP Test: for neighboring D, D′, collect N samples from M(D) and M(D′); test if observed ratio exceeds e^ε + δ with statistical significance
P9 Bun & Steinke — "Concentrated Differential Privacy" (arXiv 1605.02065, original zCDP) zCDP definition; Gaussian mechanism satisfies rho-zCDP; rho composes additively; tighter than approximate DP for repeated releases Phase 10 (ZcdpAccountant); additive composition of rho; conversion to (ε,δ): ε = rho + 2sqrt(rho·ln(1/δ)) Key advantage: no order-alpha enumeration needed; single scalar rho tracks everything
P10 Murtagh & Vadhan — "The Complexity of Computing the Optimal Composition of Differential Privacy" (TCC 2016) Optimal (exact) composition is #P-hard; RDP/zCDP are polynomial-time upper bounds on the exact composition dp-accountant: justifies use of RDP/zCDP over exact composition; tractability-vs-tightness tradeoff documented Exact composition: the optimal ε for k mechanisms is at least as small as RDP gives, but computing it is intractable
P11 Rogers et al. — "Max-Information, Differential Privacy, and Post-Selection Hypothesis Testing" (FOCS 2016) Privacy loss random variable; max-information bounds; adaptive composition generalization dp-accountant context: why RDP gives tighter bounds in adaptive composition settings than sequential (ε,δ) accounting Privacy loss RV L^(M,D,D′) = ln(Pr[M(D)=o]/Pr[M(D′)=o]); ε-DP iff L ≤ ε always
P12 Erlingsson, Pihur, Korolova — "RAPPOR: Randomized Aggregatable Privacy-Preserving Ordinal Response" (CCS 2014) Longitudinal local DP via two-stage randomized response + Bloom filters; unbiased frequency estimator; debiasing formula p̂ = (f̂ − q)/(p − q) Phase 13 dp-local: the debiasing estimator formula; heavy-hitter estimation via RAPPOR sketch Debiasing: if Pr[report=1
P13 Ghazi, Golowich, Kumar, Pagh, Velingker — "Differentially Private Quantiles" (ICML 2021) JointExp: compute multiple quantiles simultaneously with a single exponential mechanism invocation; eps-DP without divide-and-conquer bias accumulation Phase 12 dp-query quantile: use exponential mechanism for quantile, not sorted-array binary search; single-pass sensitivity analysis Sensitivity of quantile rank function = 1 under add/remove neighboring
P14 Lyu, Su, Li — "Understanding Clipping for Federated Learning" (arXiv 2021) + Steinke "Composition of Differentially Private Mechanisms" (lecture notes) Per-example clipping analysis for non-iid gradients; composition formulas for heterogeneous mechanisms dp-dpsgd: clip threshold sensitivity analysis; dp-accountant composition over mechanisms with different (ε,δ) costs Heterogeneous composition: costs tracked per-mechanism in RdpAccountant; no assumption of homogeneous cost
P15 Kamath, Li, Murtagh, Ullman — "Privately Estimating and Testing Quantiles" (STOC 2019) Lower bounds on accuracy of private quantile estimation; exponential mechanism is near-optimal dp-query quantile accuracy targets must respect these lower bounds; the exponential mechanism achieves the optimal rate Error Ω(log n / (nε)) for private median; Phase 12 accuracy specs must state this

Reference Implementations

# Repository Directories / Files to Study Architectural Lessons Pitfalls to Avoid

Framework-agnostic design
R6 Mironov CCS 2012 reference implementation (Appendix of the paper; C++ pseudocode) Algorithm 1: the snapping mechanism; lambda parameter derivation; the floor/ceiling rounding step Lambda = 2^{ceil(log2(B·ε^{−1}))} where B is the output clamp range; round the noisy value to nearest lambda multiple; this makes the output distribution on neighboring inputs indistinguishable to float-precision Lambda derivation must match the paper exactly; if lambda is too small, the output grid is finer than float representability, and the attack resurfaces. Derive from Section 5 of Mironov 2012, not from intuition
R7 StatDP/StatDP (Python; MIT) statdp/algorithms.py — reference mechanism implementations; statdp/hypothesis.py — the hypothesis test framework; statdp/core.py — neighboring dataset pair generation Hypothesis test: for S a witness event, test H0: Pr[M(D)∈S] ≤ e^ε·Pr[M(D′)∈S] + δ; p-value from binomial test; the witness event S is chosen adversarially StatDP's full adversarial witness search is expensive (randomized algorithm); dp-validate uses its statistical test methodology with fixed witness events (constructed from known mechanism structure) rather than a full search

R10 google/zetasql differential privacy SQL extension (C++; Apache-2.0) zetasql/public/functions/differential_privacy.h; zetasql/analyzer/anonymization_rewriter.cc SQL-level DP: each aggregate function injected with noise; sensitivity from column-level bounds in schema; budget checked per query block ZetaSQL's sensitivity is SQL-schema-driven; dp-query's design must similarly derive sensitivity from Bounds at query construction, not at release time

RFCs / Specifications

# Document Version / Date Relevance
S1 NIST SP 800-90A Rev 1 — Recommendation for Random Number Generation Using Deterministic Random Bit Generators Rev 1, June 2015 dp-sample CSPRNG must conform; CTR_DRBG (AES-256) or Hash_DRBG; the rand_chacha crate (ChaCha20) is an acceptable alternative under SP 800-90C; rules out rand::thread_rng() for noise generation

S6 RFC 8032 — Edwards-Curve Digital Signature Algorithm (EdDSA) Jan 2017 Secure rejection-sampling pattern in EdDSA keygen is the model for the discrete Gaussian sampler in dp-sample; constant-time rejection loop without timing side-channels
