6. Design Principles

P1 — The Privacy Guarantee Is the Contract

Statement: every value released by the engine carries a proven (ε,δ)-DP claim; the correctness of that claim is the primary success criterion.

Precise explanation:

  • DpResult<T> carries params: PrivacyParams; those params are a machine-checked contract that the distribution of value satisfies the (ε,δ) bound over any neighboring dataset pair.
  • Mechanism calibration (noise scale, sigma) is derived analytically from the DP proof, not tuned empirically.
  • Any change to a sampling algorithm requires a revised proof; statistical tests in dp-validate confirm but do not substitute.

Tradeoff: valid guarantees require more noise than an analyst might prefer; no "best-effort DP" mode exists.

Operational: Phase 14 dp-validate runs neighboring-distribution tests on each mechanism before production deployment. The CI gate blocks a release if any mechanism fails its DP test.

Violation example: a mechanism advertises (1.0, 1e-6)-DP but uses σ = l2/(ε+0.1) (a small algebraic error). The adversary running the distinguishing game wins with probability exceeding e^ε + δ. Privacy is broken silently; no runtime error fires; the engine appears to work correctly.

P2 — Sensitivity Must Be Sound (Over-, Never Under-Estimated)

Statement: the sensitivity used to calibrate noise is ≥ true global sensitivity of the (clamped) query; under-estimation is a silent privacy violation.

Precise explanation:

  • Sensitivity { l1, l2 } in dp-sensitivity must satisfy l1 ≥ sup_{D,D′ neighbors} ‖f(D)−f(D′)‖₁.
  • The ≥ is mandatory; over-estimation adds noise but preserves correctness. Under-estimation voids the privacy proof.
  • Unclamped data → sensitivity = ∞ → dp-sensitivity returns Err(UnboundedSensitivity) (I10).

Tradeoff: over-estimated sensitivity reduces utility; exact sensitivity is a bonus, not a requirement.

Operational: Phase 2 property tests fuzz Bounds and dataset pairs; assert declared sensitivity ≥ empirical max over 10k sampled pairs. Phase 14 sensitivity audit is a regression gate.

Violation example: a sum query on salaries in [0,100k] with Bounds declared as [0,1000] (programmer typo) computes sensitivity = 1000. The actual sensitivity = 100000. Noise calibrated to 1000 is 100x too small; the DP guarantee requires noise calibrated to 100000.

P3 — Secure Sampling, Not Naive Floats

Statement: all noise draws use the snapping mechanism (Laplace) or a discrete/interval-rejection sampler (Gaussian); naive IEEE 754 float-based samplers are prohibited.

Precise explanation:

  • A naive −b · ln(U(0,1)) over IEEE 754 doubles clusters probability mass at representable values near the input (Mironov, CCS 2012); an adversary recovers the exact input with O(1) queries.
  • Snapping mechanism: clamp output to [−B, B]; round to the nearest λ-grid point where λ = 2^{⌈log₂(B/ε)⌉}; add Laplace(b) noise and re-clamp. The grid structure makes the output distribution on neighboring inputs indistinguishable to float precision.
  • Discrete Gaussian sampler (Canonne et al.): samples directly over integers; no float transcendental functions in the hot path.

Tradeoff: snapping adds a λ-sized additive bias to the effective ε, which dp-sample absorbs into the advertised PrivacyParams. The overhead is negligible vs. the privacy gain.

Operational: dp-sample tests: (a) KS test of output distribution vs theoretical; (b) adjacent-input test: fix D, D′ differing by one record, sample 100k outputs from each, verify empirical ratio ≤ e^ε + δ; (c) bit-pattern test: verify no output equals the exact input in double representation.

Violation example: using rand::distributions::Standard to sample Laplace noise. An adversary queries the mechanism 50 times with the same input; the cluster of outputs at representable values near the exact input allows reconstruction with probability approaching 1. The (ε,δ)-DP guarantee is false despite correct formula usage.

P4 — Composition Is Tracked Rigorously; the Budget Is a Hard Ceiling

Statement: every mechanism invocation charges the dp-accountant; dp-budget enforces the total (ε,δ) ceiling with a hard deny; the budget cannot go negative.

Precise explanation:

  • Budget::spend(cost: PrivacyLoss) -> Result<(), BudgetError> either commits the debit atomically or returns Err(BudgetExhausted) and emits nothing.
  • No partial release; no "serve with reduced accuracy"; no silent over-spend.
  • Budget::remaining() is a read-only query available to callers for planning without charging.
  • Audit log entry written per successful spend(): { timestamp, mechanism_id, sensitivity, eps_spent, delta_spent, budget_remaining }.

Tradeoff: strict ceiling means some queries are refused when budget is nearly exhausted. Callers must plan query sequences; the engine provides remaining() for this.

Operational: I3 and I4. Phase 11 integration tests: (a) exhaust a budget in N queries, verify N+1 is denied and returns no DpResult; (b) assert budget_remaining_epsilon metric monotonically decreases; (c) crash-recovery test: reload audit log, verify budget reconstruction matches.

Violation example: a budget manager that warns but still releases results allows an analyst to query indefinitely; after k queries with budget k·ε each, the composed privacy loss is unbounded while the advertised guarantee remains ε. Every prior release is also retrospectively de-anonymized.

P5 — Post-Processing Immunity

Statement: any deterministic or randomized function of a DpResult<T> is DP with the same (or weaker) parameters; the engine never re-noises derived values.

Precise explanation:

  • Post-processing lemma (Dwork & Roth, Proposition 2.1): if M is (ε,δ)-DP and f is any function, then f∘M is (ε,δ)-DP.
  • dp-query's mean query: mean = noisy_sum / noisy_count — this is post-processing of two DP releases; no additional noise on the ratio; no additional budget charge.
  • DpResult<T> is the boundary: once a DpResult exists, transforming it is free.

Tradeoff: post-processing can only maintain or weaken the guarantee; it cannot strengthen it. Analysts may transform DpResult freely.

Operational: Phase 12 mean query tests: verify that the mean returned by dp-query has exactly the same PrivacyParams as the underlying noisy_sum and noisy_count (the tighter of the two, not their composition). Budget audit log shows a single charge for a mean query, not two.

Violation example: if the engine re-noises a ratio derived from two DP releases and charges budget again, analysts exhaust their budget twice as fast for no privacy benefit — and the second noise application adds unnecessary inaccuracy to an already-DP result.

P6 — Tighter Accounting over Looser

Statement: use RDP or zCDP accounting where it yields a smaller cumulative epsilon than basic or advanced composition; wasted budget reduces utility for no privacy benefit.

Precise explanation:

  • For k Gaussian releases with std σ and L2 sensitivity l2: basic composition gives k·ε; advanced gives ~sqrt(2k·ln(1/δ))·ε; RDP gives exact per-alpha accumulation, converted at query time to the tightest (ε,δ_target).
  • For k=1000 steps (DP-SGD), RDP/moments accountant yields ε1; advanced composition yields ε30+. The difference is the usability of the model.
  • dp-accountant exposes all three; the caller selects based on their regime; the engine defaults to RDP for Gaussian-based mechanisms.

Tradeoff: RDP/zCDP are more expensive to compute (O(|orders|) per step) and less intuitive. Justified for DP-SGD and repeated queries; overkill for a single one-shot release.

Operational: Phase 9 implements RdpAccountant; Phase 10 implements ZcdpAccountant. Phase 8 implements basic and advanced composition as fallbacks. tightest_eps_delta(delta_target) scans all alpha orders to find the minimum ε for the given δ_target.

Violation example: always using basic composition for DP-SGD training with T=1000 steps and ε=0.1 per step gives ε_total=100 — privacy is effectively zero. The analyst either stops training (zero utility) or ignores the number (privacy risk). The moments accountant gives ε_total ≈ 0.8 for the same configuration.

P7 — Clamping Is Mandatory for Unbounded Domains

Statement: real-valued data must be clamped to declared Bounds before aggregation; unclamped data has infinite global sensitivity; infinite sensitivity is refused.

Precise explanation:

  • Bounds { lower: f64, upper: f64 } defines the clamping interval. dp-sensitivity computes sensitivity as (upper−lower) for bounded sum; 1 for count; 1/n·(upper−lower) for mean under add/remove.
  • Without Bounds, global L1 sensitivity of sum over reals is ∞; dp-sensitivity returns Err(UnboundedSensitivity).
  • I6: clamping happens in dp-sensitivity's clamp(value, bounds) before any aggregation; no raw value escapes the bounds.

Tradeoff: caller must choose plausible bounds. Too-wide → over-estimated sensitivity, excess noise. Too-tight → values clipped, statistical bias introduced (a non-DP accuracy issue, not a privacy issue). The engine enforces only that the DP proof holds given the declared bounds.

Operational: dp-query constructors require Bounds; rejected at construction time, not at release. Phase 2 property tests: inject out-of-range values; verify clamp_applied_count increments and no raw value outside bounds reaches the aggregation.

Violation example: a sum query on web browsing durations with Bounds accidentally omitted. If the engine guesses sensitivity=1, it adds Lap(1/ε) noise. The true sensitivity is ∞ (one user has a 10^9-second session). The "DP" release discloses nearly exact data.

P8 — Strong Types Prevent Budget Bugs

Statement: Epsilon, Delta, Sensitivity, and noise scale are distinct newtypes; they cannot be transposed; arithmetic across them is gated at the type level.

Precise explanation:

  • Epsilon(f64), Delta(f64), Sensitivity { l1, l2 } are not interchangeable. Passing the wrong value to a noise calibration formula is a compile error.
  • Epsilon::new(v) -> Result<Epsilon, DpError> validates v > 0 and finite; Delta::new(v) validates v ∈ [0,1). No public fields; accessor methods only.
  • PrivacyParams { epsilon: Epsilon, delta: Delta } cannot be constructed with fields swapped (different types).

Tradeoff: boilerplate at construction sites; callers must wrap raw floats. Cost justified: a transposition of epsilon and delta (both f64 in a weakly-typed system) produces a mechanism that silently calibrates the wrong noise scale — the resulting guarantee is not verifiable.

Operational: Phase 1 implements all newtypes. Property tests: random f64 inputs → verify only valid inputs construct successfully. Fuzzer: generate random (f64, f64) pairs and call all constructors; assert no panic on invalid input, only Err.

Violation example: if epsilon and delta are both f64 and caller writes LaplaceMechanism::new(delta_value, epsilon_value) (transposition), the scale b = delta / sensitivity ≈ 10^{-6} / 1 ≈ 0 — essentially no noise is added; the mechanism is nearly deterministic and breaks privacy completely.

P9 — Subsampling Amplifies Privacy, but Only When Accounted

Statement: privacy amplification by subsampling is claimed only through the moments/RDP accountant that formally proves it; informal or approximate amplification claims are prohibited.

Precise explanation:

  • For Poisson subsampling with rate q from a dataset of size N: the effective privacy is amplified from (α, ε_0)-RDP to (α, ε_amplified)-RDP where ε_amplified ≤ log(1 + q·(e^{ε_0}−1)) ≈ q·e^{ε_0} for small q.
  • This proof is specific to Poisson subsampling; without-replacement (fixed batch size) requires a different bound (looser by a factor of ~2).
  • dp-dpsgd must document the subsampling scheme and use the corresponding RDP bound from dp-accountant.

Tradeoff: claiming amplification without proof produces ε lower than the true privacy loss — the advertised guarantee is false. Not claiming it produces a conservatively large ε — the model is usable but the privacy report is pessimistic.

Operational: Phase 13 dp-dpsgd tests: compare claimed ε against Opacus reference table for identical (q, σ, T, δ) parameters; tolerance ≤ 1%. Phase 14 fuzz: random (q, σ, T, N) → verify dp-accountant ε ≥ lower bound from advanced composition (sanity check on direction).

Violation example: claiming subsampling amplification for a deterministic cycling batch schedule (not Poisson). The cycling schedule does not satisfy the independence assumptions of the amplification theorem; the true ε over a cycle of N/B steps is ε_batch·N/B, not the amplified value. The model's privacy is broken relative to its advertisement.

P10 — Observability Without Leakage

Statement: the engine logs budget spend, mechanism choices, accountant state, and error conditions; it never logs raw data values or un-noised query answers.

Precise explanation:

  • Permitted in logs: mechanism type, sensitivity (declared), ε/δ cost, remaining budget, timestamp, request ID, error codes.
  • Prohibited in logs: exact query answer (pre-noise), noise draw value, any raw data field, user identifiers from the dataset being queried.
  • dp-budget audit log is append-only; each entry written after a successful spend() commit.
  • dp-cli output: only DpResult (noised answer + PrivacyParams); no debug dump of intermediate values.

Tradeoff: detailed logging aids debugging; logging any un-noised intermediate defeats the mechanism. The engine provides remaining() and audit-log replay for debugging without leaking data.

Operational: Phase 11 audit log. Phase 14 operational readiness: log review checklist verifies no exact_ prefix variables are logged. Linting rule: no tracing::debug! calls in dp-mechanism, dp-query, or dp-sample that reference pre-noise values.

Violation example: a debug log line tracing::debug!("exact_sum={}", exact_answer) before adding Laplace noise. Any party with log access (ops engineer, SIEM system, attacker with log exfiltration) reads the exact answer directly; the noise added in the next line is irrelevant.

P11 — Fail-Closed Budget

Statement: a query whose requested (ε,δ) would push the accountant's cumulative loss past the configured cap is DENIED outright; the engine never auto-renegotiates the request down to whatever budget remains and serves it anyway.

Precise explanation:

  • BudgetManager::charge / Budget::try_charge (Phase 11, dp-budget/src/budget.rs) composes the candidate cost with spent via the configured accountant before any mechanism samples noise; if the composed (ε,δ) would exceed total, the call returns Err(BudgetExhausted) and no DpResult is produced (I3, I10, I11).
  • The gate is structural, not advisory: dp-budget is the only path by which a mechanism obtains authorization to sample; there is no caller-side "try and catch" convention that can be bypassed, and no fallback that silently substitutes a smaller epsilon, a cheaper mechanism, or a coarsened query to force an over-budget request to fit.
  • Budget::remaining() is a read-only query that exists precisely so callers can plan a request that fits; the engine itself never performs that renegotiation on the caller's behalf inside the charge path.

Tradeoff: a caller who requests more privacy loss than remains gets a hard refusal, not a best-effort degraded answer; this can surprise callers used to systems that gracefully degrade, but graceful degradation here would mean an unaccounted privacy loss.

Operational: Phase 11 Budget::try_charge / BudgetManager::charge; enforced jointly with I3 (Budget Conservation) and I11 (Budget Monotonicity — cap + DENY). Phase 14 fuzzes Budget::spend for overflow/renegotiation attempts (fuzz_budget.rs).

Violation example: a budget manager that, on seeing a request for ε=0.5 against ε_remaining=0.2, quietly serves the query calibrated to ε=0.2 instead of refusing. The caller's audit trail says "charge accepted," but the mechanism used a noise scale the caller never validated or agreed to, and any downstream analyst composing on top of this silently-substituted result has no way to detect the substitution occurred.

P12 — Sensitivity Soundness (Unproven Sensitivity Is Rejected, Not Guessed)

Statement: the sensitivity used to scale noise must be a proven upper bound on the true query sensitivity; when a query's sensitivity cannot be derived and bounded — an arbitrary or composite transform with no declared stability constant — the engine rejects the query rather than substituting a guessed or empirically-estimated value.

Precise explanation:

  • P2 covers the base case: the closed-form sensitivity formulas for count/sum/mean must satisfy declared ≥ true (§8.6). P12 covers the composite case: dp-sensitivity's sensitivity_after_map(input, stability) requires an explicit TransformStability (Lipschitz(c), AddRemove, Parallel); a pipeline whose stability constant is unknown cannot be admitted on a "try it and see" basis.
  • Lipschitz(c) rejects c < 0 or non-finite c at construction (Phase 2); there is no code path that infers a stability constant empirically from sample data, because an empirically observed bound over one dataset is not a worst-case proof over all neighboring datasets.
  • Unbounded/unclamped input at any stage of a composite pipeline resolves to Err(UnboundedSensitivity) (I2, I10) even if an earlier stage in the same pipeline was properly bounded — soundness is not "mostly satisfied"; it is satisfied at every link in the transform chain or the whole query is refused.

Tradeoff: analysts building custom multi-step queries must supply and justify a stability constant for every transform; there is no "auto-detect" convenience path, which is slower than a system willing to estimate sensitivity from a data sample.

Operational: Phase 2 TransformStability / sensitivity_after_map; Phase 14's sensitivity audit (SensitivityAudit::verify_soundness) additionally fuzzes composite chains, not just base aggregates, confirming declared ≥ empirical at every stage of the chain.

Violation example: a query pipeline chains a bounded sum with a caller-supplied normalize(x) = x / observed_max transform, where observed_max is computed from the same dataset being queried. The engine cannot bound this transform's Lipschitz constant a priori — it is data-dependent — so accepting it and estimating stability from the observed data leaks information through the transform itself, independent of any noise added afterward.

7. System Invariants

Overview

Twelve invariants govern correctness, safety, and operational behavior. Each maps to one or more crates that enforce it and a detection + telemetry strategy.

ID Invariant (short form) Enforcing Crate(s) Detection Method Telemetry Key Recovery / Fail Mode
I1 DP guarantee holds: Pr[M(D)∈S] ≤ e^ε·Pr[M(D′)∈S]+δ dp-mechanism, dp-sample, dp-validate Statistical neighboring-dataset test; Phase 14 CI gate dp_mechanism_dp_test_fail_count Mechanism removed from service; calibration re-derived
I2 Declared sensitivity ≥ true global sensitivity dp-sensitivity, dp-query Property test: empirical sensitivity over 10k dataset pairs dp_sensitivity_underestimate_count (must stay 0) Query construction returns Err; no release
I3 Cumulative privacy loss ≤ total budget ceiling dp-budget Assertion: spent ≤ total after every spend(); integration tests dp_budget_remaining_epsilon, dp_budget_remaining_delta gauges; dp_budget_denied_count spend() returns Err(BudgetExhausted); nothing released
I4 Accountant cumulative (ε,δ) upper-bounds true composed loss dp-accountant Cross-check: RDP-converted ≤ basic composition (monotone sanity); reference table comparison dp_accountant_rdp_epsilon_at_order histogram Logic error; no runtime recovery; requires code fix + re-audit
I5 Sampled noise has no input-dependent representable-value gaps dp-sample KS test; adjacent-input output-distribution test; bit-pattern test dp_sample_ks_p_value gauge Sampler must be corrected; no runtime workaround
I6 All values entering aggregation lie within declared Bounds dp-sensitivity, dp-query Unit test: out-of-range inputs → verify clamped before aggregation dp_clamp_applied_count counter clamp() silently clips; logs count (not values)
I7 Post-processing: f(DpResult) is DP with same (ε,δ) dp-query, dp-core Budget audit log: derived results produce no additional spend No extra telemetry; verified by absence of double-charge N/A; remove extra noise call if detected
I8 ε > 0 finite; δ ∈ [0,1); Gaussian requires δ > 0 dp-core (Epsilon/Delta constructors), dp-mechanism Constructor returns Err on invalid; property test random f64 inputs dp_core_invalid_param_rejected_count Err returned; no partial construction
I9 Composing an additional mechanism never decreases tracked privacy loss dp-accountant (all variants) Property test: random cost sequences → assert result.ε ≥ prior.ε dp_accountant_loss_non_decreasing (debug assert) Logic error; requires code fix
I10 Unbounded sensitivity / exhausted budget / invalid params → Err, no silent release dp-sensitivity, dp-budget, dp-core Integration: trigger each failure condition; verify Err + no DpResult emitted dp_fail_closed_count per failure type Return Err; log failure type + context; zero partial output
I11 Cumulative privacy loss is non-decreasing and never exceeds the configured (ε,δ) cap; on exhaustion the engine returns DENY dp-budget, dp-accountant Accountant audit (offline ledger replay, re-derives cumulative loss from scratch) + property test (random spend sequences assert non-decreasing prefix sums ≤ cap) dp_budget_cap_violation_count (must stay 0) try_charge/spend returns Err(BudgetExhausted); zero partial commit
I12 Every mechanism draws noise from a CSPRNG with no seed reuse or observability across queries dp-sample RNG-source assertion (CI lint denies SmallRng/thread_rng/rand::random in dp-sample) + statistical test (cross-query independence / serial-correlation check) dp_sample_csprng_violation_count (must stay 0) Sampler construction fails closed; CI gate blocks release (T8)

I1 — DP Guarantee

Statement: for all neighboring D, D′ and all measurable S: Pr[M(D)∈S] ≤ e^ε · Pr[M(D′)∈S] + δ for the advertised (ε,δ).

Why it matters: this is the definition of DP; if it fails, no claim about the engine is meaningful.

Enforced in: dp-mechanism (correct calibration formula), dp-sample (secure sampler closes float-DP attack), dp-validate (statistical test).

Violation detection: Phase 14 dp-validate runs 10k samples from M(D) and M(D′) for each mechanism and tests whether the empirical ratio exceeds e^ε + δ with p-value < 0.001. Failures block CI.

Telemetry: dp_mechanism_dp_test_fail_count counter per mechanism type; alert threshold = 1.

Recovery: failed mechanism is disabled (returns Err(MechanismUntested)) until the calibration is corrected and the test passes.

Debugging: compare the analytic calibration formula against Dwork & Roth Ch. 3 / Balle & Wang theorem line-by-line; check sampler via bit-pattern test; rerun StatDP-style test with a finer grid of S.

I2 — Sensitivity Soundness

Statement: the Sensitivity { l1, l2 } used to calibrate noise satisfies l1 ≥ sup_{D,D′} ‖f(D)−f(D′)‖₁ and l2 ≥ sup_{D,D′} ‖f(D)−f(D′)‖₂ for the (clamped) query f.

Why it matters: noise is calibrated to declared sensitivity; an under-declaration adds insufficient noise, voiding the privacy proof silently.

Enforced in: dp-sensitivity computes sensitivity analytically from Bounds; dp-query wires dp-sensitivity output directly to mechanism — no caller-side override.

Violation detection: Phase 2 property tests generate 10k random neighboring dataset pairs within Bounds; assert declared ≥ empirical max. Phase 14 sensitivity audit is a CI gate.

Telemetry: dp_sensitivity_underestimate_count (expected 0 always); fires a PagerDuty alert if > 0.

Recovery: if triggered, the query is removed from service; dp-sensitivity formula is re-derived and retested.

Debugging: bisect over Bounds values to find the dataset pair that maximizes empirical sensitivity; compare against declared formula.

I3 — Budget Conservation

Statement: for any sequence of releases charged to a dataset with ceiling (ε_total, δ_total), the accountant-composed cost of all releases ≤ (ε_total, δ_total) at all times; releases that would exceed the ceiling are refused.

Why it matters: the privacy guarantee of each individual release degrades in composition; the total degradation must be bounded.

Enforced in: dp-budget: Budget::spend() checks composed cost before committing; atomic debit; no partial writes.

Violation detection: assertion spent ≤ total checked in spend() in debug builds; integration test exhausts budget in k queries and verifies query k+1 is denied.

Telemetry: dp_budget_remaining_epsilon and dp_budget_remaining_delta gauges (decreasing monotonically); dp_budget_denied_count counter.

Recovery: once denied, the caller must either accept the refusal or provision a new dataset with a fresh budget.

Debugging: replay the audit log to reconstruct budget history; verify each charge's ε/δ cost matches the mechanism's advertised PrivacyParams.

I4 — Composition Correctness

Statement: the accountant's reported cumulative (ε,δ) is an upper bound on the true composed privacy loss; it may be loose but must never be tight-in-the-wrong-direction.

Why it matters: if the accountant under-reports the composed loss, the budget enforcement in dp-budget is vacuous — I3 becomes a false guarantee.

Enforced in: dp-accountant; RdpAccountant, ZcdpAccountant, and the basic/advanced composition fallbacks.

Violation detection: sanity check: RDP-converted ε ≤ basic-composition ε (RDP is tighter; if it reports higher, the conversion has a bug). Comparison tests against Opacus/dp-accounting reference values for known (σ, q, T, δ) configurations.

Telemetry: dp_accountant_rdp_vs_basic_ratio histogram; values > 1.0 indicate a bug in the RDP-to-(ε,δ) conversion.

Recovery: no runtime recovery; a composition-correctness bug requires a code fix and a full re-audit of all budgets charged since deployment.

Debugging: isolate the conversion function tightest_eps_delta(delta_target); test against known values from Mironov 2017 Table 1 and Abadi et al. 2016 Table 1.

I5 — Secure-Sampling Fidelity

Statement: sampled noise follows the intended distribution without input-dependent representable-value gaps; the snapping/discrete sampler closes the Mironov float-DP attack.

Why it matters: a mechanism can have a correct calibration formula but a broken sampler that leaks the exact input through IEEE 754 structure.

Enforced in: dp-sample: LaplaceSampler (snapping mechanism), GaussianSampler (discrete/interval rejection).

Violation detection: (a) KS test of 10k samples vs theoretical CDF; (b) adjacent-input test: input x vs x+ULP, sample 100k from each, verify empirical ratio ≤ e^ε + δ; (c) bit-pattern test: verify no sample equals the exact input in binary representation.

Telemetry: dp_sample_ks_p_value gauge per mechanism type; alert if p < 0.01 on any nightly run.

Recovery: sampler implementation must be corrected; no runtime workaround (the attack is structural to float arithmetic).

Debugging: print the sample distribution in a logarithmic bin plot; look for spikes at representable values near 0.0 or the input value; compare bit patterns of output near the input.

I6 — Clamping Enforced

Statement: every value entering a bounded aggregate lies within [lower, upper] as declared in Bounds; out-of-range values are clamped before noise, never after.

Why it matters: a value outside Bounds contributes a sensitivity larger than declared; a single unclamped outlier can dominate the true sensitivity and break I2.

Enforced in: dp-sensitivity: clamp(value, bounds) applied in the sensitivity computation path; dp-query: each input record clamped before accumulation.

Violation detection: unit tests inject out-of-range values; assert (a) clamp_applied_count increments and (b) no raw value outside Bounds reaches the summation. Property test: fuzz inputs over [-10·upper, 10·upper] range.

Telemetry: dp_clamp_applied_count counter (non-zero values are expected and benign; sudden spikes may indicate a data pipeline issue worth investigating).

Recovery: clamping is silent and automatic; the count metric allows upstream data quality monitoring.

Debugging: if clamp_count is unexpectedly high, check upstream Bounds declaration vs actual data distribution; the Bounds may need adjustment (which will change the sensitivity and the noise level).

I7 — Post-Processing Closure

Statement: any deterministic or randomized function of a DpResult<T> is DP with the same (or weaker) parameters; the engine does not re-noise derived values or charge the budget for post-processing.

Why it matters: double-charging wastes budget and adds unnecessary noise; a single DP release suffices for any downstream transformation.

Enforced in: dp-query: derived results (mean = sum/count) documented as post-processing; dp-core: DpResult carries params immutably.

Violation detection: budget audit log inspection: each logical query appears exactly once in the audit log. Integration test: compute f(DpResult) and verify no new entry in audit log and no additional noise.

Telemetry: dp_postprocessing_double_charge_count counter (expected 0 always).

Recovery: remove the extra noise call or extra spend() call from the derived computation.

Debugging: trace the call stack of Budget::spend() to find calls triggered by post-processed derivations; verify those code paths should not be charging.

I8 — Parameter Validity

Statement: ε > 0 and finite; δ ∈ [0,1); the Gaussian mechanism requires δ > 0; pure-DP mechanisms (Laplace, exponential, randomized response) require δ = 0.

Why it matters: ε = 0 means no information released (infinite noise); ε = ∞ means no privacy (no noise); δ < 0 or δ ≥ 1 produces nonsensical probability statements. Invalid parameters produce calibration functions that overflow, produce NaN, or silently degrade to zero noise.

Enforced in: dp-core: Epsilon::new() and Delta::new() constructors; GaussianMechanism constructor rejects δ = 0; LaplaceMechanism constructor rejects δ > 0 (pure DP).

Violation detection: constructor returns Err; property test with 10k random f64 inputs verifies only the valid range constructs successfully and that no panic occurs on invalid input.

Telemetry: dp_core_invalid_param_rejected_count counter per param type.

Recovery: Err returned to caller; no partial object construction.

Debugging: log the invalid value and the construction call site; check for f64 arithmetic overflow in the caller that produced NaN or Inf before constructing the parameter.

I9 — Accountant Monotonicity

Statement: composing an additional mechanism with the accountant never decreases the tracked privacy loss: composed_cost.ε ≥ prior_cost.ε for any additional mechanism with cost > 0.

Why it matters: a non-monotone accountant could report a lower composed loss after additional queries, allowing an attacker to manipulate the accounting by inserting cheap queries to reset the counter.

Enforced in: dp-accountant: all compose() methods; asserted in debug builds.

Violation detection: property test: random sequence of positive mechanism costs; verify that the running composed loss is non-decreasing at each step. Debug assertion fires immediately on violation.

Telemetry: dp_accountant_loss_non_decreasing (debug assertion; no separate metric in production; the property test is the gate).

Recovery: non-monotone accountant is a logic error; no runtime recovery; requires code fix.

Debugging: print the per-order RDP loss vector before and after each compose(); identify the order alpha at which the loss decreased; trace to the mechanism's RDP formula.

I10 — Fail-Closed

Statement: unbounded sensitivity, exhausted budget, or invalid parameters cause a refusal (Err return); the engine never emits a DpResult with an incorrect or unverifiable guarantee.

Why it matters: a silent release with a wrong guarantee is strictly worse than a refusal; it gives false assurance and may be acted upon.

Enforced in: dp-sensitivity (Err(UnboundedSensitivity)); dp-budget (Err(BudgetExhausted)); dp-core (Err(InvalidParameter)); all return types are Result<DpResult<T>, DpError>; no unwrap() in production paths.

Violation detection: integration test triggers each failure condition; verifies Err is returned and no DpResult is emitted. Grep test: no .unwrap() or .expect() in dp-mechanism, dp-query, or dp-budget.

Telemetry: dp_fail_closed_count counter per failure type (unbounded_sensitivity, budget_exhausted, invalid_parameter).

Recovery: Err is propagated to the caller; the caller must handle (display error, adjust query, provision new budget). No partial output; no retry with modified params silently.

Debugging: match the DpError variant; log the failure context (mechanism type, declared sensitivity, budget state); correlate with audit log to determine whether the failure was expected (budget planned to be exhausted) or unexpected (sensitivity formula bug).

I11 — Budget Monotonicity (Cap + Deny)

Statement: for any dataset/analyst budget, the accountant-composed cumulative privacy loss recorded across its full history is monotonically non-decreasing and never exceeds the configured (ε_total, δ_total) cap at any point in that history; the moment a candidate charge would exceed the cap, the engine returns DENY (Err(BudgetExhausted)) and commits nothing.

Why it matters: I3 guarantees the ceiling holds at write time; I9 guarantees the accountant's per-step compose() never reports a lower loss than the prior step. I11 is the end-to-end reconciliation of both properties over the entire ledger — a bug that violates either I3 or I9 in a way that only manifests after replay (e.g., an off-by-one in ledger reconstruction after a crash) would otherwise go undetected until an audit walks the full history. I11 is the cap/DENY variant of monotonicity; I9 is the internal per-step accountant property that I11 depends on but does not replace.

Enforced in: dp-budget (BudgetLedger, Budget::try_charge, BudgetManager); dp-accountant (composition used inside try_charge).

Violation detection: accountant audit — an offline job replays every BudgetEntry in a BudgetLedger in timestamp order and re-derives cumulative loss from scratch, asserting it is non-decreasing at every prefix and never exceeds total at any prefix; property test generates random valid spend sequences (including concurrent-charge interleavings under Arc<Mutex<Budget>>) and asserts the same invariant online.

Telemetry: dp_budget_cap_violation_count (must stay 0; any nonzero value pages); dp_budget_remaining_epsilon/dp_budget_remaining_delta gauges cross-checked against the audit's reconstructed value.

Recovery: on live DENY, try_charge returns Err and nothing is committed (no partial ledger write). If the offline audit finds a historical violation, the affected dataset's budget is frozen and all releases since the divergence point are treated as unverified pending manual review.

Debugging: replay the ledger with the audit tool up to the divergent entry; compare the entry's recorded charged/remaining fields against a hand-recomputed composition using the same accountant kind; check for crash-recovery reconstruction bugs (§8.12) or concurrent-charge races bypassing Arc<Mutex<Budget>>.

I12 — CSPRNG Noise Sampling

Statement: every noise draw in dp-sample originates from a cryptographically secure RNG (OsRng-backed SecureRng); no mechanism seeds a sampler with a fixed or predictable value in production, and no two queries' noise draws are observable or correlatable by an external party.

Why it matters: I5 guarantees the distribution of sampled noise has no representable-value gaps (the Mironov float attack). I12 is a distinct, orthogonal concern: even a structurally correct sampler leaks privacy if its underlying randomness source is weak, deterministic, or reused — an adversary who can predict or observe the RNG stream across repeated queries can subtract out (average away) the noise entirely, recovering the exact answer regardless of how sound the noise distribution's shape is.

Enforced in: dp-sample (csprng.rs — OsRng wrapper is the only production SecureRng impl; MockRng is test-only and must not compile into release builds).

Violation detection: RNG-source assertion — a CI lint (custom cargo-clippy rule, T8) denies rand::random, SmallRng, or thread_rng anywhere in dp-sample, and a feature-gate check confirms MockRng is behind #[cfg(test)]; statistical test — draw noise across many independent queries from the same process and run a cross-query independence test (serial correlation / chi-square on interleaved streams) to catch accidental seed or state reuse.

Telemetry: dp_sample_csprng_violation_count (must stay 0); alert on any nonzero value.

Recovery: fail closed — if the RNG-source assertion fails at construction (wrong sampler type wired in), mechanism construction returns Err rather than falling back to a weaker default; a CI failure blocks release.

Debugging: trace which SamplerHandle/SecureRng implementation was injected at the call site; confirm dp-mechanism/src/sampler_iface.rs wiring did not fall back to NaiveSamplerHandle (Phase 3's test-only handle) in a production build; check for a fixed seed accidentally passed through configuration.

8. Domain Model

8.1 Core Entity Taxonomy

Entity Crate Cardinality Lifecycle
Dataset dp-budget one per analyst session created → queried → exhausted
Budget dp-budget 1:1 with Dataset initialized → charged N times → optionally closed
BudgetLedger dp-budget 1:1 with Budget append-only charge log
Query (trait object) dp-query many per Dataset constructed → sensitivity-checked → privatized → stamped
Mechanism (trait object) dp-mechanism instantiated per release configured → privatize(&self, exact, sens) → emits DpResult
Sensitivity dp-core / dp-sensitivity per query invocation computed or declared → validated ≥ true → consumed by mechanism
PrivacyParams dp-core per mechanism declared at construction → proven in phase tests
PrivacyLoss dp-accountant per release event produced by mechanism → composed into accountant → charged to Budget
RdpAccountant dp-accountant one per Budget (default) initialized → accumulate per release → convert to (eps,delta) on demand
ZcdpAccountant dp-accountant optional alternate rho accumulator → convert to (eps,delta)
DpResult<T> dp-core one per privatize call emitted only after budget passes; post-processing-immune
RandomizedResponse dp-local per local-DP column constructed with p → applies to each record → debiased on aggregation
DpSgdTrainer dp-dpsgd per training run per-example clip → noised batch grad → accountant charge per step

8.2 Newtype Invariant State Machine

Epsilon(f64):
  UNVALIDATED ──[f64 > 0 && finite]──► VALID
               ──[f64 ≤ 0 || NaN || Inf]──► REJECTED (Error::InvalidEpsilon)

Delta(f64):
  UNVALIDATED ──[0.0 ≤ f64 < 1.0 && finite]──► VALID
              ──[f64 < 0 || f64 ≥ 1 || NaN]──► REJECTED (Error::InvalidDelta)

Sensitivity {l1, l2}:
  UNVALIDATED ──[l1 ≥ 0 && l2 ≥ 0 && both finite]──► VALID
              ──[l1 < 0 || l2 < 0 || Inf]──► REJECTED (Error::UnboundedSensitivity)
  VALID ──[declared < true global sens]──► UNSOUND (bug; audited by dp-validate, not caught at runtime)

8.3 Budget Lifecycle State Machine

                    total: PrivacyParams
                         │
          ┌──────────────▼──────────────┐
          │  INITIALIZED                │
          │  spent = PrivacyLoss::zero()│
          └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                         │ spend(cost: PrivacyLoss)
                         ▼
          ┌─────────────────────────────┐
          │  ACTIVE                     │◄────────────────┐
          │  remaining = total - spent  │  spend() succeeds each time
          └──────┬──────────────────────┘  remaining ≥ cost
                 │
      ┌──────────┴────────────┐
      │ would_exceed_budget?  │
      │ composed_cost > total │
      └──────────┬────────────┘
                 │ YES
                 ▼
          ┌─────────────────────────────┐
          │  DENIED                     │  DpError::BudgetExhausted
          │  (no value emitted, I3)     │  fail-closed (I10)
          └─────────────────────────────┘
                 │ NO (remaining ≥ cost)
                 ▼
          ┌─────────────────────────────┐
          │  CHARGED                    │  spent += cost; ledger.append(entry)
          │  DpResult<T> emitted        │
          └─────────────────────────────┘

Atomic spend: check and update are an uninterruptible unit; no concurrent path observes a partial spend.

8.4 Privacy Accounting Type Hierarchy

PrivacyLoss (enum)
  ├── Basic(PrivacyParams)          — (eps, delta) pair from a single mechanism
  ├── Rdp(RdpCurve)                 — Vec<(alpha: f64, rdp: f64)>
  └── Zcdp(rho: f64)               — zCDP accumulator

RdpCurve
  ├── orders: Vec<f64>              — Renyi orders α > 1
  └── losses: Vec<f64>             — D_α(M(D) || M(D')) per α

Composition path:
  RdpCurve + RdpCurve  →  element-wise sum per α  (I4, I9)
  Zcdp(ρ₁) + Zcdp(ρ₂) →  Zcdp(ρ₁ + ρ₂)
  RdpCurve → PrivacyParams via: min over α of {rdp[α] + log(1/δ)/(α-1)} (Mironov 2017 conversion)
  Zcdp(ρ) → (eps,delta): eps = ρ + 2√(ρ ln(1/δ))  (Bun-Steinke)

8.5 Query Execution Sequence

  Analyst                dp-query         dp-sensitivity      dp-mechanism     dp-sample      dp-accountant    dp-budget
    │                       │                  │                  │               │                │               │
    │  execute(q, data, b)  │                  │                  │               │                │               │
    │──────────────────────►│                  │                  │               │                │               │
    │                       │ clamp(data, b.bounds)               │               │                │               │
    │                       │─────────────────►│                  │               │                │               │
    │                       │◄─ clamped_data + Sensitivity ───────│               │                │               │
    │                       │                  │                  │               │                │               │
    │                       │ exact(clamped_data)                  │               │                │               │
    │                       │──────────────────────────────────────►(computed locally)              │               │
    │                       │◄─ exact_value ──────────────────────│               │                │               │
    │                       │                                      │               │                │               │
    │                       │ privatize(exact_value, sensitivity)  │               │                │               │
    │                       │─────────────────────────────────────►│               │                │               │
    │                       │                                      │ sample_noise()│                │               │
    │                       │                                      │──────────────►│                │               │
    │                       │                                      │◄─ noise ──────│                │               │
    │                       │                                      │               │                │               │
    │                       │◄─ DpResult<T>(value, params) ────────│               │                │               │
    │                       │                                                       │                │               │
    │                       │ cost = accountant.compute(params)                     │                │               │
    │                       │──────────────────────────────────────────────────────►│                │               │
    │                       │◄─ PrivacyLoss ───────────────────────────────────────│                │               │
    │                       │                                                                        │               │
    │                       │ budget.spend(cost)                                                     │               │
    │                       │───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────►│               │
    │                       │◄─ Ok(()) | Err(BudgetExhausted) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────│
    │◄── DpResult<T> ───────│  (only if Ok)                                                                         │

8.6 Sensitivity Computation Rules (Normative)

Query Neighboring relation L1 global sensitivity L2 global sensitivity
Count add/remove one record 1 1
Bounded sum ([l,u]) add/remove max(|l|, |u|) max(|l|, |u|)
Bounded mean ([l,u], n) add/remove (u-l)/n (u-l)/n
Histogram (k bins, [l,u]) add/remove 1 (parallel: max bin sens=1) 1
Quantile (via exp mech) add/remove 1/n (score sensitivity) —
  • Replace neighbor model: sensitivity may differ; user must declare which model is in use.
  • Unbounded query (no Bounds declared) → Error::UnboundedSensitivity (I10, P7).
  • Transform stability: if a map f has stability t (i.e., d(f(D),f(D')) ≤ t·d(D,D')), then sensitivity of g∘f = t · sens(g).

8.7 Mechanism Calibration Normative Rules

Mechanism Noise law Scale / sigma rule DP guarantee
Laplace Lap(b) b = l1_sens / ε pure ε-DP (δ=0)
Gaussian (classical) N(0,σ²) σ = l2_sens · √(2 ln(1.25/δ)) / ε (ε,δ)-DP; not tight
Gaussian (analytic, Balle-Wang) N(0,σ²) σ = smallest s.t. Φ(Δ/(2s) - εs/Δ) - e^ε·Φ(-Δ/(2s) - εs/Δ) ≤ δ (ε,δ)-DP; optimal σ
Exponential Categorical P(o) ∝ exp(ε·u(o)/(2·Δu)) ε-DP
Report-noisy-max Lap(1/ε) per score b = 1/ε (sensitivity 1 counts) ε-DP (cheaper than Exp for argmax)
Randomized response (local) Bernoulli(p) p = e^ε/(1+e^ε) ε-LDP; debiased estimator unbiases the aggregate

Invariant: noise scale must be computed from the sensitivity declared at call time, not any cached value (I2).

8.8 Sparse Vector Technique (SVT) Model

SVTState:
  threshold: T (noised once at construction, pays 1 use from ρ_threshold)
  above_count: u32
  above_limit: u32   (k = max above-threshold queries to answer)
  epsilon_threshold: Epsilon
  epsilon_answer: Epsilon   (charged per ABOVE answer; no charge for BELOW)

Transitions per query q_i:
  noised_q_i = q_i(D) + Lap(2/eps_threshold)
  if noised_q_i < threshold: emit ⊥ (BELOW); no budget charge for answer
  else if above_count < above_limit:
    emit true + Lap(4k/eps_answer)  (ABOVE); charge eps_answer / k to budget; above_count++
  else: terminate SVT (all k answers exhausted)

Privacy: total cost = epsilon_threshold + epsilon_answer (sequential composition of the two components); parallel composition does NOT apply here.

8.9 DP-SGD Execution Model

Per training step t:
  for each example i in minibatch B:
    g_i = ∇L(θ, x_i)                           // per-example gradient
    g_i_clipped = g_i / max(1, ‖g_i‖₂ / C)    // clip to L2 norm ≤ C
  g_sum = Σ g_i_clipped
  g_noised = (g_sum + N(0, σ²C²·I)) / |B|      // σ = noise_multiplier
  θ ← θ - η · g_noised

Accountant charge per step:
  q = |B| / N  (subsampling rate)
  Compute RDP(α) = subsampled_gaussian_rdp(α, σ, q) per order α
  Accumulate into RdpAccountant
  Privacy budget checked after each step; training halts if budget exhausted (I3, I10)

Key parameters:
  C:  gradient clipping norm (bounds L2 sensitivity per example = C)
  σ:  noise multiplier (σ·C = Gaussian std dev)
  q:  subsampling rate → amplification; ONLY claimed through RDP accountant (P9)

8.10 Serialization / Trace / Wire Layouts

Audit log entry (append-only, stored as newline-delimited JSON):

{
  "ts": "<RFC3339>",
  "dataset_id": "<uuid>",
  "query_id": "<uuid>",
  "mechanism": "<LaplaceMechanism|GaussianMechanism|...>",
  "sensitivity": {"l1": <f64>, "l2": <f64>},
  "privacy_cost": {"epsilon": <f64>, "delta": <f64>},
  "accountant_type": "<RDP|zCDP|Basic>",
  "budget_remaining": {"epsilon": <f64>, "delta": <f64>},
  "outcome": "CHARGED" | "DENIED"
}

Logged fields (P10): mechanism type, sensitivity, cost, outcome. NOT logged: raw data, exact query result, noise value.

PrivacyParams wire encoding (Protobuf schema sketch):

message PrivacyParams {
  double epsilon = 1;   // must be > 0, finite; validated on decode
  double delta   = 2;   // must be in [0,1); 0 implies pure DP
}
message RdpCurve {
  repeated double alpha = 1;
  repeated double loss  = 2;  // parallel arrays; same length required
}

CLI output format (dp-cli):

{
  "value": <T>,
  "epsilon": <f64>,
  "delta": <f64>,
  "mechanism": "<string>",
  "budget_remaining_epsilon": <f64>
}

Raw exact value never appears in CLI output (P10).

8.11 Worked Example — Bounded Sum Query

Setup: dataset D of salaries, n=1000, bounds [20000, 200000], total budget ε=1.0, δ=1e-6.

  1. Bounds { lower: 20000.0, upper: 200000.0 } → declared.
  2. dp-sensitivity: l1_sens = max(|20000|, |200000|) = 200000; l2_sens = 200000.
  3. LaplaceMechanism { scale: 200000 / 1.0 = 200000.0 } — Laplace noise with scale 2e5.
  4. dp-sample (snapping): compute exact sum, add Lap(2e5) via snapping mechanism (not naive float).
  5. DpResult { value: exact_sum + noise, params: PrivacyParams { epsilon: 1.0, delta: 0.0 } }.
  6. RdpAccountant.accumulate(rdp_laplace(alpha, 1.0)) for each order α.
  7. Budget.spend(cost) → remaining ε reduces; ledger entry appended.
  8. Analyst receives numeric answer + PrivacyParams stamp; raw sum never emitted.

If the analyst prefers Gaussian: delta=1e-6 → analytic Gaussian σ calibration via Balle-Wang bisection → sigma ≈ 200000 · sqrt(2 ln(1.25/1e-6)) / 1.0 ≈ 739000 (classical), or tighter analytic sigma ≈ 715000 → RdpAccountant tracks D_α for each order; accountant is charged before emitting the result.

8.12 Failure-Path Example — Budget Exhaustion Mid-Session

Setup: budget ε=0.5, δ=0. Three Laplace queries each requesting ε=0.2.

Step Cost Cumulative (basic) Budget check Outcome
Q1 ε=0.2 0.2 0.2 ≤ 0.5 → PASS CHARGED; DpResult emitted
Q2 ε=0.2 0.4 0.4 ≤ 0.5 → PASS CHARGED; DpResult emitted
Q3 ε=0.2 0.6 0.6 > 0.5 → FAIL DENIED; Err(BudgetExhausted); nothing emitted

Q3 leaves no partial state change: the budget read and the check are atomic; no noise was sampled before the check passes (fail-closed, I3, I10).

8.13 Mechanism Privacy Proof Sketches (Normative Constraints)

Each mechanism must satisfy the following normative constraints — not full proofs, but the key inequality the implementation must honor:

Laplace Mechanism

  • Output: f(D) + Lap(b), b = Δ₁/ε.
  • Proof constraint: for any D, D' neighboring, any S ⊆ ℝ: Pr[M(D) ∈ S] / Pr[M(D') ∈ S] ≤ exp(|f(D)-f(D')|/b) ≤ exp(Δ₁/b) = exp(ε).
  • Implementation check: b > 0 (ε > 0 && Δ₁ > 0); b = ∞ rejected (I8, I10).

Gaussian Mechanism (analytic)

  • Output: f(D) + N(0, σ²).
  • Proof constraint (Balle-Wang): σ is the smallest value satisfying Φ(Δ₂/(2σ) - εσ/Δ₂) - e^ε · Φ(-Δ₂/(2σ) - εσ/Δ₂) ≤ δ.
  • Implementation check: δ > 0 (pure DP impossible for Gaussian, I8); bisection tolerance ≤ 1e-10.

Exponential Mechanism

  • Output: candidate o with probability ∝ exp(ε · u(o) / (2·Δu)).
  • Proof constraint: for any neighboring D, D', any output o: Pr[M(D)=o] / Pr[M(D')=o] = exp(ε(u(D,o)-u(D',o))/(2Δu)) ≤ exp(ε).
  • Implementation check: utility differences bounded by Δu (sensitivity of u); log-sum-exp stable normalization.

Randomized Response

  • Output: true answer with prob p = e^ε/(1+e^ε); flip with prob 1-p.
  • Proof constraint: p/(1-p) = e^ε; satisfies ε-LDP.
  • Debiasing estimator: p̂_true = (p̂_obs - (1-p)) / (2p-1); unbiased, variance inflated by 1/(2p-1)².

8.14 Composition Proof Constraints

Composition type Constraint enforced by accountant Reference
Basic sequential (Σεᵢ, Σδᵢ) is a valid upper bound Dwork-Roth Thm 3.14
Advanced sequential (k mechanisms, each ε) (ε√(2k·ln(1/δ')) + k·ε(e^ε-1), δ'+k·δ) Dwork-Roth Thm 3.20
Parallel (disjoint subsets) max(εᵢ) for ε; max(δᵢ) for δ Dwork-Roth
RDP sequential RDP[α] = Σ RDP_i[α] per order; convert at end Mironov 2017 Prop 1
zCDP sequential ρ_total = Σ ρᵢ Bun-Steinke Lem 1.8
Subsampled Gaussian (DP-SGD) RDP amplified by subsampling rate q Mironov 2017 Thm 9

Accountant must choose the tightest applicable rule at conversion time (P6).

8.15 RDP Accountant Detail

Per-mechanism RDP loss (α > 1):

Mechanism D_α(M(D) ‖ M(D'))
Gaussian N(0,σ²), Δ₂ α·Δ₂²/(2σ²)
Laplace Lap(b), Δ₁ min(α·Δ₁²/(2b²), ...) — exact formula via moment generating function
Subsampled Gaussian (rate q) ≤ (1/α-1)·log(q²·D_α/(M_q)+...) — Mironov Thm 9

Conversion RDP → (ε,δ):

  • For each order α: ε(α,δ) = rdp[α] + log(1/δ)/(α-1).
  • Return min over α { ε(α,δ) } for the target δ.
  • Implementation: sweep orders vector, return minimum; order vector should include [1.5, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64] as defaults.

Composition accumulation invariant (I9):

  • After k steps: rdp_total[α] = Σᵢ rdp_i[α] for all α.
  • Monotone: adding step k+1 can only increase rdp_total[α] (δ ≥ 0 per-step contribution).

8.16 Edge Cases

Edge Case Behavior
ε=0 passed to Epsilon::new Err(InvalidEpsilon); construction fails
δ=1.0 passed to Delta::new Err(InvalidDelta); construction fails
Sensitivity l1=0 (constant query) Valid; noise scale = 0; mechanism adds no noise (deterministic release charges ε=0)
Gaussian requested with δ=0 Err(PureDpIncompatible); Gaussian requires δ>0 (I8)
Bounds [u, l] where l > u Err(InvalidBounds)
Data value outside [lower, upper] Clamped silently to nearest bound (I6); NOT an error
SVT above_limit reached Further queries return Err(SvtExhausted); no budget charged
RDP orders Vec is empty Err(InvalidRdpOrders); accounting refuses to proceed
Concurrent spend on same Budget Serialized via internal mutex; second spend sees post-first state
DP-SGD minibatch size > dataset size Err(InvalidSubsamplingRate); q must be in (0,1]
Composition of RDP + Basic PrivacyLoss Forced conversion of Basic → RDP before summation; or convert RDP→(ε,δ) and sum as Basic (less tight)
Snapping mechanism overflow (large exact value) Clamp exact value to representable range before snap; documented precondition
Histogram with 0 records in a bin Count = 0; sensitivity still 1 (add/remove); noise still added to 0
Mean query with n=0 Err(EmptyDataset); sensitivity undefined for 0-record mean
Quantile p=0 or p=1 Exponential mechanism over sorted candidates; edge candidates bounded by declared Bounds
Multiple analysts sharing one Budget Named sub-budgets; total partitioned at creation; sub-budgets cannot borrow from each other
RDP order α=1 Approaches KL divergence; 0/0 limit excluded from order vector; skip or handle via L'Hopital
RDP curve with all losses=0 Convert yields ε=0; only valid when sensitivity=0 (deterministic query); flagged in dp-validate
Epsilon very large (e.g., 1e15) Accepted by Epsilon::new; noise approaches 0; privacy is technically satisfied but semantically useless; dp-cli may warn

8.17 Type Ownership and Lifetime Model

Key ownership rules enforced at compile time:

Relationship Ownership model Rationale
Budget and BudgetLedger Budget owns BudgetLedger exclusively Ledger is an internal implementation detail; no outside ref
RdpAccountant inside Budget Budget owns accountant; exposes borrow for read Prevents out-of-band accountant mutation
DpResult<T> returned by mechanisms Owned value returned to caller Post-processing immunity: caller may transform freely (I7)
Mechanism trait objects Arc<dyn Mechanism> for shared-mechanism configs Multiple queries may share a configured mechanism
Query trait objects Box<dyn Query> per invocation Single-use; no shared state between query executions
Dataset reference Borrowed &[Record] passed to query Engine never takes ownership of raw data; borrow ends after query
Bounds Copy (two f64 fields) Passed by value to avoid lifetime complexity in sensitivity path

Concurrency: Budget wraps inner state in Mutex<BudgetInner>; Arc<Budget> is Send + Sync. All other core types are Send (no raw pointers). RdpAccountant accumulation is single-threaded within the mutex.

8.18 Local DP Frequency Estimation Model

Objective: estimate frequency vector f ∈ [0,1]^k from n local DP responses without accessing true answers.

Randomized Response on k categories:

  • Each user holds true answer v ∈ {1,...,k}.
  • Report true v with prob p = (e^ε/(e^ε + k - 1)); report each other value with prob (1-p)/(k-1).
  • Satisfies ε-LDP per user.
  • Aggregator receives n reports; computes empirical frequency of each category.
  • Debiased estimate: f̂_j = (p̂_j - (1-p)/(k-1)) / (p - (1-p)/(k-1)).
  • Variance per cell: O(k e^ε / (n (e^ε-1)²)).

Heavy-hitter sketch (RAPPOR-style):

  • User encodes v into a Bloom filter; applies randomized response per bit.
  • Server decodes candidates above frequency threshold from noisy counts.
  • Privacy: ε-LDP per user (bit-level RR with per-bit ε' summing to ε via basic composition across bits).

8.18 dp-validate Statistical Test Protocol

Neighboring dataset test (per mechanism M):

  1. Fix a query f and two neighboring datasets D, D' (differ by one record).
  2. Sample N=10000 outputs from M(D) → distribution P; N=10000 from M(D') → distribution Q.
  3. For each threshold t, compute empirical Pr[M(D) ≥ t] and Pr[M(D') ≥ t].
  4. Check: for all t, log(Pr[M(D) ≥ t] / Pr[M(D') ≥ t]) ≤ ε + δ_slack.
  5. δ_slack = 1/√N (statistical tolerance); fail if exceeded by more than 3σ.
  6. Also check the reverse direction (symmetry of DP).

Sensitivity audit:

  • For n=1000 random pairs (D, D') that differ by one record, compute |f(D) - f(D')|.
  • Assert: max over pairs ≤ declared_sensitivity.l1 (soundness, I2).
  • If any pair exceeds declared, emit SensitivityViolation error.

Accountant cross-check:

  • Run k=100 identical Gaussian releases; compare RDP-accountant's (ε,δ) vs advanced composition (ε,δ).
  • Assert: RDP gives ε ≤ advanced-composition ε (tightness check, P6).
  • Assert: both ≥ true composition lower bound from first-principles (correctness check, I4).

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (stable channel, MSRV 1.78) C++, Go, Python Memory safety without GC; strong type system for newtype invariants; zero-cost abstractions; no undefined behavior for numeric code; portfolio consistency Python wins for research prototyping where the NumPy/diffprivlib ecosystem and rapid iteration beat performance and safety
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspace with per-crate Cargo.toml Bazel, Buck Cargo is the de-facto Rust build system; workspace deduplicates deps; minimal toolchain surface area Bazel wins in a polyglot monorepo with remote build caching across many languages and thousands of targets
ADR-03 Dependency policy Minimal, pinned, audited (cargo audit); no network deps at runtime liberal transitive deps Privacy engine must be auditable; each dep is a potential attack surface; cargo-vet policy for crate reviews Liberal deps win for a fast-moving research library where breadth of functionality matters more than an auditable supply chain
ADR-04 CSPRNG source rand_chacha (ChaCha20) seeded from OS entropy via getrandom rand default, thread_rng, hardware RDRAND alone ChaCha20 is deterministic, audited, and portable; OS entropy (getrandom) is the trusted seed; avoids RDRAND-only dependency (NIST concern); required for I5 Hardware-RDRAND-alone wins on constrained embedded targets that lack an OS entropy path, accepting the single-source trust tradeoff
ADR-05 Laplace sampler Snapping mechanism (Mironov CCS 2012) — clamp to λ·Z, snap to nearest floating-point multiple of λ Naive inverse-CDF on f64 Naive float Laplace leaks exact input through representable-value gaps; snapping closes the attack; mandatory for P3, I5 Naive inverse-CDF wins in a non-adversarial teaching/demo context where the floating-point attack is explicitly out of scope
ADR-06 Gaussian sampler Discrete Gaussian (Canonne-Kamath-Steinke NeurIPS 2020) or analytic Gaussian with interval refinement Box-Muller on f64, Ziggurat Continuous Gaussian on floats has bias; discrete Gaussian is exact over integers; analytic for real-valued with documented precision bounds; closes float-DP gap (I5) Box-Muller/Ziggurat win for fast continuous Gaussian in non-privacy uses (simulation, initialization) where exactness is irrelevant
ADR-07 Analytic Gaussian calibration Balle-Wang (ICML 2018) tight σ via bisection on the error-function equation Classical σ = Δ·√(2 ln(1.25/δ))/ε Classical bound wastes ~10–30% σ; analytic is minimax optimal; bisection converges in <50 iterations; justified in ADR narrative The classical closed form wins when you need a one-line, dependency-free σ and can absorb 10–30% extra noise (quick estimates)
ADR-08 Privacy accountant (default) RDP accountant (Mironov CSF 2017) with configurable order vector Basic composition only, zCDP only RDP gives strictly tighter (ε,δ) than advanced composition for DP-SGD; additive per-order composition is O(k·|orders|); zCDP available as alternate; basic is fallback for Laplace/Exp Basic composition wins for a handful of Laplace/exponential queries where RDP machinery is overkill and the looser bound is acceptable
ADR-09 zCDP support ZcdpAccountant (Bun-Steinke TCC 2016) alongside RDP Merge into RDP only Some mechanisms (pure Gaussian) are naturally expressed in zCDP; separate accumulator avoids lossy conversion at each step; conversions defined in dp-accountant RDP-only wins when every mechanism is Gaussian-family and a single accumulator simplifies the API and avoids dual book-keeping
ADR-10 Serialization format serde_json for CLI / audit log; optional prost Protobuf for inter-service wire CBOR, MessagePack, custom binary JSON is human-readable for audit logs (required by P10); Protobuf for service integration is standard; no custom binary to avoid reimplementing encoding bugs CBOR/MessagePack win when audit-log volume is huge and compact binary + decode speed beat human-readability
ADR-11 Audit log format Newline-delimited JSON (NDJSON), append-only file SQLite, structured syslog Append-only = tamper-evident; NDJSON is grep-friendly; no DB dependency in the core engine; rotation via log agent (logrotate / Vector) outside the crate SQLite wins when audit logs need rich queryable indexes and transactional integrity on a single node
ADR-12 Concurrency model Synchronous by default; Arc<Mutex<Budget>> for shared mutable budget; no async runtime in core Tokio async throughout, Rayon only DP core is compute-bound, not I/O-bound; async adds complexity; Budget mutex is the only shared state; dp-cli may wrap in Tokio for CLI I/O Tokio-async wins when the engine is embedded in an async service fielding many concurrent network-bound DP queries
ADR-13 Parallelism for DP-SGD rayon data-parallel per-example gradient clipping Manual threads, ndarray-linalg Per-example clip is embarrassingly parallel; Rayon's work-stealing is safe with Rust ownership; no shared mutable state per example Manual threads or a GPU backend win when integrating with an existing thread pool or accelerator that Rayon cannot target
ADR-14 Numeric linear algebra ndarray for tensor ops in dp-dpsgd nalgebra, faer, raw BLAS ndarray has a mature Rust ecosystem; faer considered for future; avoid C FFI in core numeric path to maintain safety guarantees Raw BLAS/faer win for large dense linear algebra where peak FLOPs dominate and the C-FFI risk is acceptable
ADR-15 Error handling thiserror for structured error types per crate; anyhow in dp-cli only Box<dyn Error>, custom string errors thiserror produces typed, match-able errors; anyhow is acceptable in binary crates; typed errors are required for programmatic budget-denial handling anyhow wins in binary/CLI code where call-site ergonomics beat programmatic match-on-variant
ADR-16 Property-based testing proptest for newtype invariant, composition, and sensitivity soundness tests quickcheck, manual fuzz proptest has richer shrinking and strategy composition; mandatory for I1–I10 coverage; custom proptest strategies for valid (ε,δ) pairs quickcheck wins for a tiny test surface where its simpler model suffices and proptest's strategy machinery is unnecessary
ADR-17 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) on parser, bounds, mechanism constructors, accountant inputs AFL via afl.rs, honggfuzz cargo-fuzz integrates with cargo and CI; targets: malformed PrivacyParams, extreme sensitivity values, adversarial RDP order vectors honggfuzz/AFL win when their instrumentation reaches deeper coverage on a specific hard-to-fuzz target
ADR-18 Statistical DP validation Custom neighboring-dataset hypothesis tests in dp-validate, modeled on StatDP (Ding et al. CCS 2018) Differential tester only, no statistical test Statistical tests catch calibration bugs invisible to unit tests (e.g., wrong σ by 0.01%); StatDP approach: sample M(D) and M(D'), run KS / chi-squared test on ratio of empirical CDFs A pure differential tester wins as a fast pre-commit smoke test, reserving the expensive statistical suite for release gates
ADR-19 Benchmarking criterion with criterion-perf-events for cycle counting std::time only, hyperfine Criterion gives statistical significance; perf-events adds IPC data; benchmarks run in CI on tagged releases; targets: sample throughput, accounting overhead per release hyperfine wins for quick end-to-end CLI wall-clock comparisons without writing a bench harness
ADR-20 Logging tracing crate with tracing-subscriber (JSON formatter in production) log + env_logger, slog tracing is structured, span-aware, and composable; JSON formatter feeds the audit pipeline; spans capture query lifecycle without logging raw data (P10) log + env_logger win for a minimal CLI where structured spans add no value
ADR-21 Metrics metrics crate facade + metrics-exporter-prometheus in dp-cli OpenTelemetry SDK, custom counters metrics facade allows backend swap; Prometheus is the standard scrape target; counters: budget_spend_total, budget_denied_total, mechanism_invocations OpenTelemetry wins when the deployment standardizes on OTLP traces + metrics across a distributed system
ADR-22 CI/CD GitHub Actions: cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy, cargo fmt --check, cargo audit, fuzz job (10-min budget), criterion regression check Jenkins, CircleCI GitHub Actions is zero-ops; matrix over stable + MSRV; fuzz in CI catches regressions before merge Jenkins wins inside enterprises with self-hosted runners and compliance constraints GitHub-hosted runners cannot meet
ADR-23 Packaging Library crates published to crates.io; dp-cli as a standalone binary via cargo install Docker-only distribution, npm/pip wrapper Rust library should be composable; dp-cli binary gives standalone tooling; Docker image for dp-cli provided for server deployments A pip/Docker-only wrapper wins when the primary users are Python data scientists who never touch Cargo
ADR-24 Container debian:bookworm-slim base for dp-cli Docker image; multi-stage build (builder: rust:1.78, runtime: slim) Alpine (musl edge cases with CSPRNG), distroless bookworm-slim has glibc (avoids musl getrandom edge cases); multi-stage keeps image small; getrandom syscall access verified in CI distroless wins when minimal attack surface is paramount and the musl/getrandom edge cases are independently mitigated
ADR-25 Reproducibility Pinned Cargo.lock checked in; cargo vendor for offline build; MSRV pinned in rust-toolchain.toml Floating deps, no lockfile Reproducibility is required for security review and auditability; pinned deps allow cargo audit to be meaningful Floating deps win for a canary CI lane whose purpose is to surface upstream breakage as early as possible
ADR-26 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (SPDX dual license) GPL, proprietary Dual MIT/Apache-2.0 is the Rust ecosystem norm; compatible with OpenDP and Google-DP reference implementations; no GPL viral risk for library users GPL wins when copyleft is a deliberate strategy to force downstream openness; proprietary when the DP IP is a commercial moat
ADR-27 Sensitivity declaration model Programmer-declared Bounds + engine-computed sensitivity (not automated sensitivity tracking via type-level proof) Full automated sensitivity type system (as in OpenDP's Rust DSL) Automated tracking is powerful but dramatically increases API complexity and compilation time; declared+audited model is simpler, more approachable, auditable via dp-validate; noted as a future evolution path Automated type-level sensitivity tracking wins in high-assurance settings where correctness-by-construction justifies the API complexity
ADR-28 Floating-point determinism Best-effort: snapping mechanism normalizes float representation; tests tolerance-bounded not bit-exact Require bitwise reproducibility across platforms True bitwise repro across x86/ARM requires disabling FPU optimizations; snapping already reduces float-space to a discrete grid; statistical tests use tolerance bands Bitwise reproducibility wins when regulation mandates identical output across machines for audit, accepting the FPU-optimization loss
ADR-29 Sensitivity declaration vs. type-level proof Programmer-declared Bounds checked at runtime + audited by dp-validate Full type-level proof system (OpenDP-style Haskell/Rust DSL) Type-level proofs are correct-by-construction but significantly raise API complexity and compilation time; declared+validate is pragmatic, auditable, and composable A full proof system wins for formally-verified deployments that must ship a machine-checked privacy proof with every release
ADR-30 Budget-per-dataset vs. global budget Per-dataset Budget with optional per-analyst sub-budget Single global budget for all queries Datasets have independent privacy guarantees; per-dataset budgets enforce isolation (I3); sub-budgets allow multi-analyst deployments without one analyst exhausting another's quota A single global budget wins for a single-tenant, single-dataset deployment where isolation is irrelevant and simplicity matters
ADR-31 Neighboring relation model Add/remove (unbounded DP) as default; replace (bounded DP) opt-in via API flag Replace-one only, or both silently Add/remove is the conservative default (higher sensitivity); replace-one gives lower sensitivity for mean/sum but requires different proof; caller must declare; sensitivity depends on the choice Replace-one (bounded DP) as default wins when all analyses are fixed-size means/sums and the lower sensitivity is worth the bounded-DP proof
ADR-32 Sparse vector technique isolation Dedicated dp-svt crate with its own SVTState; not merged into dp-mechanism Merge into dp-mechanism SVT has unique stateful semantics (above_limit, mixed threshold/answer epsilon); isolation prevents misuse of privacy accounting (only one threshold cost, answer costs are conditional) Merging SVT into dp-mechanism wins for a minimal build needing only one or two SVT queries that wants fewer crates
