Alerting Philosophy

  • Page on invariant violations: I3 (budget exceeded), I5 (sampler failure, dp_validate_test_pass=0), I8 (invalid params spike), I10 (fail-closed path triggered).
  • Page on dp_ledger_write_conflict_total > 0 — potential double-spend.
  • Page on dp_audit_log_write_errors_total > 0 — fail-closed budget path.
  • Warn at budget_spent_pct > 80%; page at > 95%.
  • Alert on budget denial rate spike (> 10/min per analyst): possible probing (T2, T6).
  • Alert on dp-validate test failure — possible sampler regression (F5).
  • Deduplication: group by dataset_id; suppress repeated param_invalid from same analyst for 5 min after first alert.
  • No alert on normal query flow; metrics emitted silently.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement Window Error Budget
Query latency P99 (non-DP-SGD) < 10 ms 5-min rolling 0.1% of requests
Budget denial correctness 100% of over-budget queries denied Continuous 0 violations
Sampler correctness (dp-validate) All tests pass on each release build Per-build CI 0 failures to prod
Audit log durability 0 lost entries under crash Quarterly chaos test 0 lost entries
Budget ledger monotonicity 0 decrements observed Continuous invariant 0 violations
Param rejection correctness 100% of invalid params rejected Continuous property test 0 violations

Incident Response

Incident Trigger Immediate Investigation Resolution
Budget integrity dp_ledger_write_conflict_total > 0 or budget anomaly Freeze new queries for dataset_id; alert data steward Replay audit log; compare to snapshot Patch race condition; re-derive spent from log; unfreeze after verification
Sampler failure dp_validate_test_pass = 0 for any mechanism Quarantine mechanism; halt releases using it Bisect sampler code; review LSB test output Patch dp-sample; full Phase 14 validation before re-enable
Sensitivity soundness breach Sensitivity audit in dp-validate: declared < empirical Quarantine affected Bounds config; halt affected query type Determine scope of affected releases Re-issue with correct Bounds; notify data owners; document affected period
Accountant undercount Cross-check fails in dp-validate Mark affected sequences suspect; use conservative basic composition as fallback Identify wrong RDP formula; compute true spend Patch accountant; recalculate and re-apply budget charges for affected sequence
Audit log disk full dp_audit_log_write_errors_total > 0 All queries fail-closed (budget charge refused) Check disk usage; identify retention policy violation Free space; rotate logs; resume; no charges lost (all denied during outage)

Runbooks

Runbook Trigger Key Steps
RB-01: Budget reset Budget exhausted; new analysis period (1) Operator confirms with two-step CLI prompt; (2) Append RESET event to audit log with operator_id + timestamp; (3) Atomically zero spent; (4) Notify analyst; (5) Verify ledger monotonicity invariant after reset
RB-02: Ledger replay Corruption detected (F6); post-incident audit (1) Lock dataset_id (deny all queries); (2) Read append-only audit log from beginning; (3) Recompute spent from event stream; (4) Compare to current snapshot; (5) Patch discrepancy; (6) Unlock after verification; (7) Report to data steward
RB-03: Mechanism quarantine dp-validate failure (F5) (1) Disable mechanism in dp-cli config; (2) Drain any in-flight queries; (3) Patch dp-sample; (4) Run full dp-validate test suite; (5) Review release build; (6) Re-enable via config after CI gate passes
RB-04: Sensitivity audit T1 suspected; F2 anomaly; pre-publication review (1) Run dp-cli validate sensitivity --query <type> --bounds <B>; (2) Compare declared l1/l2 to empirical range over representative data; (3) If declared < empirical: widen Bounds, re-issue query; (4) Mark prior releases as potentially under-noised
RB-05: DP-SGD early stop Budget ceiling approached mid-training (1) Check dp_dpsgd_epsilon_total vs ceiling; (2) If remaining < 1-epoch worth: stop training; (3) Log final (eps,delta) and training step; (4) Evaluate partial model utility; (5) Decide: publish partial model or re-tune sigma/clip_norm and restart with new budget
RB-06: Supply-chain incident cargo-audit CVE in dep (1) Pin to patched version in Cargo.lock; (2) Re-run cargo-deny; (3) Rebuild; (4) Re-run full sampler validation and dp-validate; (5) Deploy; (6) Audit whether prior releases used compromised crate
RB-07: Analyst token suspension Budget probing detected (T2, T6) (1) Revoke analyst token; (2) Freeze associated dataset_id; (3) Audit last N query log entries for pattern; (4) Report to data steward; (5) Restore access only after review
RB-08: Audit log disk full dp_audit_log_write_errors_total > 0 (1) All queries fail-closed during outage (no data loss — all charges refused); (2) Free disk: rotate or archive old log segments; (3) Verify log integrity on restart (SHA-256 chain); (4) Resume; note: no charges were lost, all denied

Pre-Release Operational Checklist

Each release build must pass the following gates before promotion to stable:

Gate Owner Tooling Pass Criterion
dp-validate Phase 14 statistical conformance dp-validate Empirical neighboring-dataset test All mechanisms pass at configured alpha level
Sampler LSB uniformity test dp-validate Chi-squared on LSB distribution p-value > 0.05 for 10^6 samples
Accountant cross-check dp-validate RDP → (eps,delta) vs advanced composition Difference < 1e-9 for test sequences
Budget monotonicity property test Proptest spent_after >= spent_before for all sequences 10,000 random sequences pass
Sensitivity soundness property test Proptest declared_l1 >= empirical_l1 10,000 random bounded datasets pass
Parameter validity exhaustive test Proptest All newtype constructors reject invalid values 100% boundary cases covered
Cargo audit clean CI cargo-audit + cargo-deny Zero known CVEs in dep tree
Sampler determinism under fixed seed Unit test dp-sample with explicit seed Output bit-for-bit reproducible
Budget denial correctness Integration test Over-budget sequence refused; under-budget allowed 100% of test cases pass
CLI flag stability Snapshot test dp-cli --help output No flag removed relative to prior release

Deployment Topology

  • dp-core through dp-validate: library crates; linked into host binary; no network service; no daemon.
  • dp-cli: single statically-linked binary; invoked per-query or as a session REPL.
  • Budget ledger: embedded by default (file-backed with Mutex + append-only audit log); BudgetStore trait allows externalisation to transactional KV store for multi-process deployments.
  • Multi-process deployment: budget ledger must be externalised (e.g., to a transactional KV store implementing BudgetStore); dp-budget provides the trait; caller supplies the impl.
  • No TLS, no RPC in the core engine; network boundaries are the caller's responsibility.
  • Containerized deployment: single binary + ledger volume mount; no privileged access required; audit log volume must have fsync capability.
  • On-premises / air-gapped: vendored deps supported via cargo vendor; no outbound network calls from the engine itself.

Canary / Rolling Upgrade / Rollback / Migration

Operation Procedure
Canary Route 5% of queries to new binary; compare dp_mechanism_noise_scale, dp_validate_test_pass, and latency across canary vs stable; promote after 30-min soak
Rolling upgrade Stateless compute (mechanisms, accountant); flush/snapshot budget ledger before upgrade; new binary reads existing audit log on startup
Rollback Replace binary; audit log is forward-compatible (append-only, versioned); spent re-derived from log on startup; no schema migration for rollback within a MAJOR version
MAJOR version migration Run dp-cli budget migrate --from v1 --to v2; idempotent; verified by ledger replay; requires maintenance window; old binary must be drained before new binary activates
Audit log rotation Rotate daily; archive to off-site store; retain 90 days; SHA-256 chain integrity verified on rotation

Compatibility Guarantees

Semantic Versioning Policy

Component MAJOR MINOR PATCH
Public API types (dp-core, dp-budget, dp-accountant, dp-mechanism, dp-query) Breaking type/trait/field change New mechanism / accountant / query type; new optional field Bug fix; sampler correctness; performance
Audit log schema Breaking field removal or rename New optional field; new event_type value Format fix only
CLI flags and JSON output Breaking flag removal or output schema change New subcommand or flag Fix only
dp-dpsgd, dp-validate Breaking API change Additive Fix
  • Pre-1.0: 0.y.z; MINOR may break API with changelog notice.
  • Post-1.0: MAJOR required for any breaking change in dp-core, dp-budget, dp-accountant.

API Stability Tiers

Crate Tier Notes
dp-core Stable (post-1.0) Epsilon, Delta, PrivacyParams, Sensitivity, DpResult frozen; only additive changes
dp-budget Stable Budget, BudgetStore trait frozen; new methods additive
dp-accountant Stable RdpAccountant, ZcdpAccountant frozen; new accountant types additive
dp-mechanism Stable Mechanism trait frozen; new mechanism structs additive
dp-sensitivity Stable Sensitivity computation function signatures stable
dp-query Stable Query trait and built-in query type signatures stable
dp-local Stable RandomizedResponse stable post-1.0
dp-svt Stable SparseVectorTechnique stable post-1.0
dp-sample Internal No public API stability guarantee; accessed only via dp-mechanism
dp-dpsgd Experimental API may change between minor versions until 1.0
dp-validate Experimental Test API surface subject to change
dp-cli Stable (flags + JSON) Subcommand flags and --json output format stable post-1.0; api_version field in JSON output

Wire / Trace Compatibility

  • DpResult<T> JSON serialization: fields value, params.epsilon, params.delta, query_id; new optional fields may be added (readers ignore unknown — open world); no field removal without MAJOR bump.
  • Trace span schema: new attributes may be added with MINOR bump; existing attributes stable post-1.0.
  • Audit log: JSON lines; schema_version field required; new optional fields with MINOR bump; event_type enum values never removed (new values added with MINOR bump); chained SHA-256 integrity hash field.
  • DpResult<T> bincode (binary) encoding: field order and discriminant values frozen within MAJOR version; new fields appended only; bincode v2 with explicit field tagging.
  • RPC / IPC: the engine has no built-in RPC; callers embedding the engine in a service own the wire format; DpResult<T> JSON schema is the recommended interchange format.

Guaranteed-Stable Type Signatures (post-1.0)

The following function signatures are frozen — any change is a MAJOR bump:

dp-core:
  Epsilon::new(f64) -> Result<Epsilon, ParamError>
  Delta::new(f64) -> Result<Delta, ParamError>
  PrivacyParams::new(Epsilon, Delta) -> PrivacyParams

dp-budget:
  Budget::new(PrivacyParams) -> Budget
  Budget::spend(&mut self, PrivacyLoss) -> Result<(), BudgetError>
  Budget::remaining(&self) -> PrivacyLoss

dp-mechanism (Mechanism trait):
  fn privatize(&self, exact: T, sens: Sensitivity) -> Result<DpResult<T>, MechanismError>
  fn privacy_params(&self) -> PrivacyParams

dp-query (Query trait):
  fn sensitivity(&self, bounds: &Bounds) -> Result<Sensitivity, SensitivityError>
  fn exact(&self, data: &[f64]) -> f64

dp-accountant:
  RdpAccountant::new(orders: &[f64]) -> RdpAccountant
  RdpAccountant::compose(&mut self, mechanism_rdp: &[f64])
  RdpAccountant::to_privacy_params(&self, delta: Delta) -> Result<PrivacyParams, AccountantError>

Storage / Format Compatibility

Artifact Format Evolution Rule
Audit log JSON lines Additive fields only; schema_version required; readers skip unknown fields
Budget ledger snapshot bincode (default) or JSON MAJOR version change for breaking struct change; migration tool dp-cli budget migrate provided
RDP curve (RdpAccountant serialized state) JSON array [{"order": f64, "rdp": f64}] Additive only; crate semver tag in envelope
zCDP state (ZcdpAccountant) JSON {"rho": f64} Additive only; crate semver tag in envelope
CLI --json output JSON with api_version field MAJOR bump on breaking schema change; MINOR for new fields
DpResult bincode bincode v2 MAJOR bump on type change; migration utility for persisted results

Type / Schema Evolution Rules

  • PrivacyParams: epsilon and delta fields frozen; new fields (e.g., rho: Option<f64> for zCDP) as Option with default None; deserialization must accept absent optional fields.
  • Sensitivity: l1 and l2 frozen; new norm fields (e.g., L∞) as Option.
  • Bounds: lower and upper frozen; new fields (e.g., step: Option<f64> for discrete domains) as Option.
  • Budget: total and spent frozen; sub-limit fields additive.
  • Enum variants: never removed from public enums; new variants added with MINOR bump; public match arms use #[non_exhaustive] to force caller catch-all.
  • Trait methods: new methods added with default implementations (no breaking change); methods without defaults require MAJOR bump.

Deprecation Policy

  • Deprecated items annotated #[deprecated(since="X.Y.Z", note="use X instead")]; retained for at least one full MAJOR version.
  • Deprecated CLI flags: retained for one MAJOR version; emit a WARN log on use; listed in CHANGELOG under [Deprecated].
  • Deprecated accountant methods (e.g., basic composition superseded by RDP for Gaussian): retained until MAJOR bump; replacement advertised in docs.
  • No silent removals: all removals appear in CHANGELOG under [Removed] with migration path.

Migration Guarantees

Migration Guarantee Tool
Audit log v0.x → v1.0 All prior entries remain valid; schema_version header backfilled dp-cli audit migrate --to v1
Budget ledger across MAJOR Idempotent migration; verified by ledger replay post-migration dp-cli budget migrate --from vN --to vN+1
RDP accountant state across MINOR No migration needed; new orders appended; existing orders preserved N/A
DpResult<T> deserialization across MINOR Forward-compatible: new fields ignored; backward-compatible: missing optional fields use defaults N/A
DpResult<T> across MAJOR Migration utility for persisted results; schema diff documented in CHANGELOG dp-cli result migrate

Rolling-Upgrade Guarantees

  • During rolling upgrade (old + new binary coexist): audit log entries from both versions are valid; schema is additive and version-tagged.
  • Budget ledger: MINOR upgrades — both versions can read/write; MAJOR upgrades — maintenance window required; drain old binary before activating new.
  • Mechanisms: a DpResult from the old binary is a valid, readable result in the new binary within a MAJOR version; (eps,delta) semantics unchanged.
  • Cross-crate version constraint: dp-core version must be identical across all crates in a compiled binary (Cargo workspace enforces via resolver); no mixed-version dp-core in a single binary.

Cross-Portfolio Compatibility

Portfolio Project Interface Point Compatibility Guarantee
#407 Federated Learning / Secure Aggregation Consumes DpResult noise parameters; dp-dpsgd integrates via moments accountant PrivacyParams JSON schema stable post-1.0; moments accountant RDP output format stable
#392 MPC dp-mechanism may be invoked inside MPC circuit; needs deterministic noise from shared seed Sampler accepts explicit seed in MPC mode (separate from production OsRng path); API stable post-1.0
#120 RLHF / Training Pipelines DP-SGD noise injection and per-step budget tracking dp-dpsgd API stable post-1.0; per-step DpResult output format versioned with api_version
#15 Synapse / #112-120 ML stack dp-query histogram / bounded sum consumed by downstream analytics Query JSON output with api_version field stable post-1.0; schema evolution additive only

Invariant-to-Compatibility Mapping

The following table ties system invariants (section 7) to compatibility commitments, ensuring that no version bump silently weakens a DP guarantee:

Invariant Compatibility Commitment Detection if Broken
I1 (DP guarantee) Mechanism (eps,delta) advertised in DpResult matches actual distribution; any tightening of the guarantee is backward-compatible; any weakening requires MAJOR bump dp-validate Phase 14 statistical test; CI gate
I2 (sensitivity soundness) Sensitivity computation for a given Bounds is deterministic and version-stable; changes that reduce declared sensitivity require MAJOR bump Sensitivity property tests; cross-version comparison
I3 (budget conservation) Budget denial semantics (deny if spend would exceed ceiling) are frozen; no version may introduce a "warn but serve" mode without a MAJOR bump Budget monotonicity test; integration test suite
I4 (composition correctness) Accountant formula for a given mechanism type is stable within a MAJOR version; improvements that reduce reported epsilon are backward-compatible; formula corrections that increase reported epsilon require a migration note Accountant cross-check in CI
I5 (secure-sampling fidelity) Sampler type used for each mechanism is stable within a MAJOR version; switching from snapping to a different Laplace sampler requires MAJOR bump and re-validation Sampler type label in audit log; dp-validate LSB test
I8 (parameter validity) Validity rules (eps > 0, delta in [0,1)) are frozen; no version may accept previously-rejected params without MAJOR bump Property tests; newtype constructor fuzz
I10 (fail-closed) All fail-closed paths (unbounded sensitivity, exhausted budget, invalid params) produce Err, never Ok with a degraded guarantee; any relaxation is MAJOR Fail-closed integration tests in CI

Testing Strategy

Unit Testing

Target Method Key Assertions
Epsilon/Delta newtypes Boundary construction tests eps<=0 -> Err; delta>=1 -> Err; NaN/inf -> Err
Sensitivity arithmetic Exhaustive signed values l1,l2 >= 0; over-estimation preserved under composition
Bounds clamping Value tables lower < upper; input outside -> clamped; invariant I6
LaplaceMechanism::privatize Scale = l1/eps formula sigma == sens.l1 / epsilon; scale > 0
GaussianMechanism analytic sigma Cross-check vs Balle-Wang formula sigma(Balle-Wang) <= sigma(classical); delta=0 -> Err
ExponentialMechanism softmax Fixed utility tables log-sum-exp stability; uniform input -> uniform output
RandomizedResponse debiasing Known p=0.75 case debiased estimator converges to true freq in expectation
RdpAccountant per-order addition Gaussian RDP = alpha/(2*sigma^2) composition = element-wise sum; monotone (I9)
ZcdpAccountant rho accumulation Known sigma -> rho = l2^2/(2*sigma^2) additive under composition; convert rho -> (eps,delta)
Budget::spend boundary Exactly-at-ceiling case at ceiling -> Err; below -> Ok; negative impossible (I3)
DpResult<T> post-processing Apply arbitrary fn params unchanged; value transforms freely (I7, P5)

Each crate: #[cfg(test)] modules, no I/O, deterministic seeds via ChaCha8Rng::seed_from_u64(FIXED).

Property-Based Testing (proptest + quickcheck)

  • Sensitivity soundness (I2): forall clamped dataset D, sensitivity(query, D, bounds) >= true_sensitivity(query, D) — generate random datasets, bounds, query types; assert declared >= empirical.
  • Composition monotonicity (I9): spending additional budget never decreases PrivacyLoss; generate sequences of heterogeneous mechanism charges; assert partial sums are non-decreasing.
  • Clamping idempotency: clamp(clamp(x, B), B) == clamp(x, B) for all x, B.
  • Budget conservation (I3): sum of all approved PrivacyLoss charges never exceeds ceiling; exhaustive random charge sequences including failures.
  • RDP-to-(eps,delta) conversion: for any (eps,delta) produced by RDP conversion, the direct Gaussian mechanism with same params satisfies the (eps,delta) definition empirically (cross-check against dp-validate).
  • Snapping mechanism output range: Laplace samples drawn through snapping never produce exact input (no zero-noise event).
  • ExponentialMechanism selection frequency: chi-squared test that selection distribution matches exp(eps*u / 2*sens) proportionality over 50k samples.

Integration Testing

Scenario Crates Crossed Pass Criterion
Full query pipeline: clamp -> sensitivity -> Laplace -> budget charge dp-sensitivity, dp-mechanism, dp-budget, dp-query Budget decreases exactly by claimed eps; DpResult carries correct params
DP count query repeated k times, advanced composition dp-query, dp-accountant, dp-budget Advanced composition bound tighter than basic after k>=10
Histogram over 100 bins, parallel composition dp-query, dp-accountant Total eps = max(per-bin eps), not sum
DP-SGD 3 epochs, moments accountant dp-dpsgd, dp-accountant, dp-budget Epsilon tracked per step; total matches moments formula at epoch end
Budget exhaustion denial dp-budget, dp-query (k+1)-th query denied when k queries exhaust budget; no partial release
CLI round-trip: dp-cli query count --eps 1.0 dp-cli, all downstream Exit 0; JSON output contains params.epsilon == 1.0; budget reduced
SVT above-threshold stream dp-svt, dp-accountant Stops after first k above-threshold answers; budget charged once for setup
Local DP frequency estimation dp-local Debiased estimate within 3*sigma of true freq at n=10k

Differential (Reference) Testing

Reference: OpenDP (opendp/opendp) and Google DP library (Java/Go) as oracles.

Mechanism Oracle Comparison Metric
Laplace noise scale OpenDP make_laplace sigma within 1e-10 (floating-point budget)
Analytic Gaussian sigma Balle-Wang reference Python (ICML 2018 code) sigma within 1e-8
RDP Gaussian at orders [2,4,8,16,32] Tensorflow-Privacy compute_dp_sgd_privacy per-order divergence within 1e-6
Advanced composition bound Google DP AdvancedComposition eps within 1e-9
moments accountant (DP-SGD) Abadi et al reference (CCS 2016 code) eps within 0.01 at 100 steps

Test harness: cargo test --test differential reads fixture JSON produced by oracle scripts; asserts near-equality.

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libfuzzer)

Fuzz Target Corpus Seed Invariant Checked
fuzz_params_construction All invalid (eps,delta) combinations No panic; invalid -> Err; valid -> Ok
fuzz_sensitivity_analysis Random datasets + bounds No panic; sensitivity >= 0; no UB
fuzz_mechanism_privatize Random (sensitivity, eps, delta, exact value) No panic; output finite; scale > 0
fuzz_accountant_compose Sequences of random PrivacyParams No panic; cumulative loss monotone
fuzz_budget_spend Random charge sequences No panic; never negative budget; over-limit -> Err
fuzz_cli_input Malformed JSON / UTF-8 Exit nonzero; no panic; no partial release

Run 30 minutes per target in CI on merge to main; RUSTFLAGS=-Zsanitizer=address enabled.

Statistical DP Conformance (dp-validate, StatDP-style)

Protocol per Ding et al (CCS 2018):

  1. Generate 200k samples from M(D) and M(D') where D, D' differ by one record.
  2. Compute empirical likelihood ratio; test that Pr[M(D) in S] / Pr[M(D') in S] <= e^eps + delta for all S using binomial proportion test at 99.9% confidence.
  3. Mechanisms tested: LaplaceMechanism, GaussianMechanism, ExponentialMechanism, RandomizedResponse, ReportNoisyMax.
  4. Sensitivity audit: inject an under-declared sensitivity (declared < true); assert dp-validate flags it.
  5. Accountant cross-check: compare RDP->（eps,delta) conversion output vs advanced composition output for same sequence; assert RDP is no looser.

Runs as a nightly CI job (not every PR; too slow). Results written to validation-report.json; CI fails if any mechanism fails its DP test.

Fault Injection and Chaos

  • Inject Delta(0.0) into GaussianMechanism -> must Err (I8, I10).
  • Inject Sensitivity { l1: f64::INFINITY } -> LaplaceMechanism must refuse (I10).
  • Replace CSPRNG with a biased stub in dp-sample; assert dp-validate detects the distribution deviation.
  • Simulate concurrent Budget::spend from 8 threads; assert no double-spend (budget never goes negative).
  • Kill a budget ledger mid-commit (via panic injection); assert the ledger rolls back and the release is denied.

Soak and Regression Testing

  • Soak: run 1M Laplace samples; test KS statistic against theoretical CDF < 0.01.
  • Perf regression: criterion benchmarks for each mechanism; CI fails if p50 latency regresses >10%.
  • Determinism: given a fixed CSPRNG seed, two runs of any mechanism produce identical output (required for replay and auditability).

Workspace Structure

[workspace]
members = [
  "dp-core", "dp-sensitivity", "dp-sample", "dp-mechanism",
  "dp-svt", "dp-accountant", "dp-budget", "dp-query",
  "dp-local", "dp-dpsgd", "dp-validate", "dp-cli"

dp-core has zero non-std dependencies; all others may depend on dp-core; dependency DAG is acyclic.

Dependency Auditing

  • cargo audit on every CI run; blocks merge on any RUSTSEC advisory with severity >= medium.
  • cargo deny checks licenses (allow: MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause; deny: GPL, SSPL, non-commercial).
  • cargo deny checks duplicate dependency versions; bans.deny for known vulnerable crates.
  • cargo update --dry-run weekly via Dependabot; automerge patch updates, require review for minor/major.

SBOM and Provenance

  • cargo-cyclonedx generates CycloneDX SBOM on every release; attached to GitHub Release as sbom.xml.
  • cargo-auditable embeds dependency tree in the compiled binary (audit-info ELF section).
  • Binaries signed with cosign (keyless, Sigstore); provenance attestation via slsa-github-generator SLSA level 3.
  • sha256sum of every release artifact committed to checksums.txt in the release tag.

CI/CD Topology

on: [push to main, pull_request]
jobs:
  check         — cargo check --all-targets
  test-unit     — cargo test --workspace (unit + integration)
  test-fuzz     — cargo fuzz run <targets> --max-total-time=30 (30 min, main only)
  test-validate — statistical DP tests (nightly schedule only)
  audit         — cargo audit + cargo deny
  bench-ci      — cargo bench --workspace -- --save-baseline pr; compare vs stored main baseline
  clippy        — cargo clippy -- -D warnings
  fmt           — cargo fmt --check
  sbom          — cargo cyclonedx (release tags only)
  cross-build   — cross build --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl --release
  publish       — cargo publish (release tags; gated on all above passing)

Gate: all jobs except test-validate and test-fuzz must pass for PR merge.

Version Stamping

  • CARGO_PKG_VERSION from Cargo.toml; semver, dp-core version is the engine version.
  • All crates in the workspace share a single version number (synchronized via cargo-workspaces).
  • git describe --tags --dirty embedded at compile time via build.rs in dp-cli.
  • dp-cli --version emits dp-engine 0.x.y (commit abc1234, built 2026-06-06T00:00:00Z).

Throughput and Latency Targets

Operation Target p50 Target p99 Notes
Laplace sample (snapping) < 500 ns < 2 µs CSPRNG-bound
Gaussian sample (discrete) < 2 µs < 10 µs Rejection sampling loops
RDP composition (100 orders) < 5 µs < 20 µs Vector dot-product bound
Budget::spend (single-threaded) < 1 µs < 5 µs Mutex-free fast path
Count query (1M rows) < 50 ms < 200 ms Scan-dominated
Histogram (1k bins, 1M rows) < 100 ms < 400 ms Parallel composition overhead minimal
DP-SGD step (batch=256) < 10 ms < 40 ms Gradient clip dominated

Profiling Methodology

  1. Flamegraph: cargo flamegraph --bench <name> (perf on Linux, DTrace on macOS); identify hot crates.
  2. Sampling profiler: samply for macOS; perf record -g + perf report for Linux.
  3. Instruction profiling: cargo-asm to inspect generated code for noise-sampling hot paths; verify no implicit heap alloc in inner loop.
  4. CSPRNG cost isolation: benchmark ChaCha8Rng::fill_bytes(64) standalone; verify it is < 30% of total sample cost.

Allocator Analysis

  • dhat (via dhat-rs) on query_count_pipeline benchmark: assert < 5 allocations per query after warmup.
  • jemalloc optional feature for server deployments; default to system allocator.
  • Inner loops in dp-sample and dp-mechanism::privatize must be allocation-free; enforced by #[no_alloc] annotation (or cargo-no-alloc lint) on hot paths.

Lock Analysis and Concurrency

  • Budget uses std::sync::Mutex<BudgetState>; contended path profiled with parking_lot::Mutex as alternative.
  • Lock hold time target: < 1 µs (budget ledger update only; accountant work done outside the lock).
  • cargo-thread-sanitizer (RUSTFLAGS=-Zsanitizer=thread) on integration tests to detect data races.
  • Lock-free alternative for read-only budget checks: AtomicU64 approximate ceiling check before acquiring the mutex (fast rejection path).

Tracing and Observability

  • tracing crate with structured spans; level DEBUG for mechanism choices, INFO for budget charges, WARN for near-exhaustion, ERROR for denials.
  • tracing-subscriber JSON formatter for machine-readable audit log.
  • P10 (observability without leakage): spans carry mechanism name, epsilon charged, remaining budget — never raw data or exact query result.
  • opentelemetry integration optional feature (dp-cli --features otel); exports spans to OTLP collector.

Cache and Memory Profiling

  • L1/L2 cache pressure: RDP accountant iterates over Vec<f64> of alpha-orders; keep orders Vec < 256 elements (fits in cache line cluster).
  • valgrind --tool=massif on 10M-sample soak; assert peak RSS < 50 MB.
  • Histogram bins: pre-allocated Vec<f64> of size num_bins; no reallocation during query execution.

Scalability Analysis

Dominant Scaling Pressures

This system is a per-query noise engine, not a distributed store. Its scaling pressures are:

Pressure Driver Ceiling
Budget ledger contention Concurrent analysts issuing queries Single-node mutex; ~100k q/s at 8 threads before contention dominates
RDP accountant vector size Number of distinct alpha-orders tracked Memory and per-composition CPU linear in `
Exponential mechanism candidate set Number of candidates per query `O(
DP-SGD per-example clipping Model parameter count * batch size CPU O(batch * params); GPU-offload needed for > 10M params
dp-validate statistical tests Sample count for power 200k samples per mechanism-neighbor pair; parallelizable, not on critical path
Audit log growth Query volume Append-only; rotate at 1 GB; reads not on query path

Horizontal Scaling

  • dp-query / dp-mechanism / dp-sample: stateless per-query; scale horizontally behind a router; each instance holds a shard of the budget ledger.
  • Budget ledger sharding: shard by (dataset_id, analyst_id); cross-shard queries require a distributed transaction or a coordinator.
  • Accountant state: per-dataset RDP curve is O(|orders| * num_mechanisms) floats; fits in memory for up to ~1M mechanisms per dataset; beyond that, compact representation (prune dominated orders).
  • dp-validate: embarrassingly parallel per mechanism; run as separate worker pool, not on query path.

Vertical Scaling

  • Laplace/Gaussian sampling: SIMD-vectorized ChaCha8Rng::fill_bytes benefits from wider AVX2 (4x f64 lanes); negligible above 8 cores for single-query workloads.
  • RDP accountant: SIMD addition over the alpha-orders vector; auto-vectorized by LLVM at opt-level=3.
  • Memory: each analyst session holds one Budget struct (~200 bytes) and one RdpAccountant (~128 * 8 = 1 KB for 128 orders); 100k concurrent sessions = ~125 MB.

Coordination Overhead

  • Per-query critical section: Mutex::lock -> read spent -> call accountant -> update spent -> unlock. Hold time ~500 ns; throughput ceiling at 8 threads ~ 16M ns / (8 * 500 ns) ≈ 4k q/s per dataset shard (Amdahl-limited).
  • Mitigation: pipeline the accountant computation before the lock; enter the lock only for the compare-and-swap of spent.
  • Distributed mode: a two-phase spend protocol — (1) speculative debit, (2) confirm or rollback — adds one round-trip; target < 1 ms for intra-datacenter.

Bottleneck Inventory

Bottleneck At Scale Mitigation
Budget mutex contention > 1k concurrent queries per dataset Shard budget by analyst; lock-free approximate pre-check
Discrete Gaussian rejection loop Low-sigma (high-eps) regimes: many rejections Clamp sigma from below; fall back to Laplace when eps >> 1
Exponential mechanism with large candidate set > 100k candidates Streaming log-sum-exp; avoid materializing full probability vector
RDP vector operations > 256 alpha orders Prune dominated orders after each composition step
dp-validate 200k-sample test Per-release validation Run async, post-release; not on query critical path
Audit log I/O > 1M q/s Async append via tokio::io::AsyncWriteExt; batch writes in 4 KB pages

Scaling Ceilings

Resource Ceiling Reached When
Budget ledger (single node) ~50k q/s 8 cores, 500 ns/op, 50% mutex hold
RDP accountant memory 1 MB per dataset 128 orders * 1M composed mechanisms
Laplace sample throughput ~4M samples/s CSPRNG-bound (ChaCha8, AVX2)
Gaussian sample throughput ~500k samples/s Rejection sampling; sigma-dependent
Audit log disk ~1 GB/hour at 1M q/s Rotate and archive; not a query-path bottleneck

Growth model: budget ledger state is O(1) per dataset (fixed-size struct); accountant state is O(num_compositions * |orders|) per dataset. At 1M queries/day per dataset, accountant state reaches ~1 MB/day; prune after each snapshot to bound it at a constant.

Mental Models & First Principles

The compressed insights an expert holds before writing a line. Each names what it predicts, the bug class you write without it, and the section where this blueprint applies it.

MM1 — DP is a property of the mechanism, not of the output. ε-DP bounds how much one record can shift the output distribution: for neighboring D, D′, Pr[M(D)∈S] ≤ e^ε · Pr[M(D′)∈S] + δ. Predicts: you cannot look at one release and decide whether it is "private" — privacy lives in the randomized algorithm and its parameters. Bug class without it: treating a single noised number as "anonymized" and reusing it freely. Applied in: §8 Domain Model, Phase 1, Phase 3.

MM2 — Noise is calibrated to sensitivity, and getting sensitivity wrong silently voids the guarantee. Laplace scale = sensitivity/ε; sensitivity is the worst-case change in the query output when one record changes. Predicts: underestimating sensitivity gives too little noise, so the proven ε is a lie no test will flag. Bug class: computing sensitivity on the realized data instead of the worst case, or skipping clamping (making it unbounded). Applied in: Phase 2, I6, I10, §4.4.

MM3 — Floating-point sampling leaks; the snapping mechanism is the fix. Naive inverse-CDF Laplace on f64 only lands on representable values; the gaps reveal the exact input (Mironov CCS 2012). Predicts: a correct-on-paper sampler is attackable in practice. Bug class: mu + laplace_sample() from a library RNG with no snapping/clamping. Applied in: ADR-05, P3, I5, Phase 5.

MM4 — Composition is the budget: privacy degrades as you query, and the accountant is the ledger. k queries compose to ≈kε (basic) or ≈√k·ε (advanced); RDP/zCDP give tighter bounds. Predicts: an unbounded query stream has unbounded privacy loss — you MUST stop. Bug class: answering queries without charging a budget, or mis-counting composition. Applied in: Phase 8, Phase 9, Phase 10, I9.

MM5 — RDP linearizes composition; convert to (ε,δ) only once, at the end. Rényi DP tracks a curve over orders α; composing is adding per-order RDP, and a single conversion at report time gives the tightest (ε,δ). Predicts: converting after every step loses tightness and inflates the reported ε. Bug class: per-step (ε,δ) conversion. Applied in: ADR-08, Phase 9, §4.5, MM4.

MM6 — The budget ledger must be fail-closed and atomic, or it is not a guarantee. Spend is a single atomic check-and-charge; on exhaustion the engine errors, never returns a default/zero answer. Predicts: a TOCTOU gap between check and charge lets two concurrent queries overspend. Bug class: if budget.remaining() > cost { release(); budget.spend(cost) } (non-atomic). Applied in: ADR-12, ADR-30, Phase 11, I10, §4.3.

MM7 — Post-processing is free; any function of a DP output is still DP. The post-processing theorem means debiasing randomized-response counts, rounding, or clamping a noised answer costs no additional budget. Predicts: transforms of an already-private value are free. Bug class: re-charging budget for a pure transform, wasting budget. Applied in: Phase 7, Phase 13, MM4.

MM8 — DP-SGD privacy = subsampling + clipping + noise, accounted by the moments/RDP accountant. Per-example gradients are L2-clipped (bounding sensitivity), Gaussian noise is added to the batch sum, and subsampling amplification is tracked across thousands of steps. Predicts: clipping after averaging, or ignoring subsampling amplification, breaks the bound. Bug class: clipping the mean gradient instead of per-example gradients. Applied in: ADR-13, Phase 13, MM4, §4.1.

Comparative Architecture Table

This design versus real differential-privacy systems. Real systems only.

Dimension This design (dp-engine) OpenDP / SmartNoise Google DP / PipelineDP Opacus Tumult Analytics
Language / runtime Rust core + CLI Rust core, Python/R bindings C++ core, Go/Java; PipelineDP on Beam/Spark Python on PyTorch Python on Apache Spark
Sensitivity model Declared Bounds, engine-computed, audited (ADR-27) Type-level stability/transformation calculus (correct-by-construction) Per-aggregation bounded contributions Fixed L2 clip norm Per-aggregation with truncation
Default accountant RDP (Mironov 2017); zCDP alt Composition via privacy measures Basic/advanced + PLD RDP / PRV accountant PLD / RDP
Secure sampling Snapping Laplace + discrete Gaussian (I5) Snapping / discrete Secure discrete noise Gaussian on floats (ML tolerance) Platform noise
Scope Query + DP-SGD + local DP Query + transformations Query + data pipelines DP-SGD only SQL-style relational queries
Budget enforcement Fail-closed per-dataset ledger Measurement chaining; caller tracks Per-pipeline budget Accountant, no ledger Per-query budget on Spark

OpenDP / SmartNoise shares this engine's Rust core. Its distinguishing decision is a type-level transformation/measurement calculus that makes sensitivity correct-by-construction rather than declared-and-audited. That wins in high-assurance settings where the type system should prove sensitivity — exactly the regime ADR-27/ADR-29 cite as the rejected alternative's home.

Google DP library / PipelineDP is a C++ core with Go/Java wrappers plus a Beam/Spark pipeline layer. Its distinguishing decision is scale-out over data-processing pipelines. That wins for petabyte-scale aggregations across a cluster where DP must ride on existing Beam/Spark infrastructure.

Opacus is PyTorch-native DP-SGD only. Its distinguishing decision is deep autograd integration to capture per-sample gradients efficiently. That wins when the sole goal is private deep learning on PyTorch and query-style DP is irrelevant — the niche this engine reaches only at Phase 13.

Tumult Analytics is Spark-based relational DP. Its distinguishing decision is a SQL-like relational API with a privacy-aware query planner. That wins for analysts running relational aggregations on Spark who want DP without touching mechanism internals.

IBM diffprivlib is a Python/NumPy library of drop-in DP versions of scikit-learn models. Its distinguishing decision is ML-ecosystem ergonomics. That wins for rapid research prototyping in Python — the regime ADR-01 names when rejecting Python for the core.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

Every number is derived from the §4 System Constraints tables with the formula shown.

C1 — Single noise-draw throughput. §4.1: <1 µs p99/draw. Formula: 1 / 1 µs = 10⁶ draws/s per core per thread.

C2 — Histogram query cost. §4.1: 1000-bin histogram <1 ms; each bin is one draw (C1). Formula: bins × draw_cost = 1000 × 1 µs = 1 ms. Confirms parallel composition — disjoint bins are independent.

C3 — End-to-end query overhead. §4.1: dp-query <10 µs vs draw <1 µs. Formula: query = draw + sensitivity_calc + budget_spend; the ~9 µs balance is sensitivity computation + the <5 µs atomic budget charge (C4).

C4 — Budget-ledger throughput. §4.1: atomic spend <5 µs p99. Formula: 1 / 5 µs = 2×10⁵ spends/s per dataset (single CAS sequence, §4.3).

C5 — Accountant state size. §4.2: <100 KB per accountant at 100 orders. Formula: |orders| × 8 B = 100 × 8 = 800 B per composed curve; pruning bounds the total (Scalability Analysis).

C6 — RDP compose cost. §4.1: 50-order compose <20 µs (a Vec<f64> add). Formula: |orders| ops, memory-bound. 10⁴ mechanisms → 10⁴ × 20 µs = 0.2 s total accounting.

C7 — DP-SGD per-step overhead. §4.1: <100 µs/step at batch ≤256 (excl. backprop). Formula: step = 256 per-example clips + 1 Gaussian draw on the batch gradient + 1 accountant charge.

C8 — DP-SGD memory. §4.2: <512 MB at batch 256, 1M params; engine adds one noise buffer. Formula: caller_grad_buffer + 1 × (1M params × 8 B) = +8 MB over the caller's allocation.

C9 — Audit-log rotation point. §4.2/§4.6: <10 MB ledger, ~64 B/entry, structural cap 1M entries. Formula: max_RAM_entries = 10 MB / 64 B ≈ 156,000. Rotation triggers at ~156 K entries — well before the 1M structural cap. (Surfaces the real tension between the two constraints.)

C10 — Conformance-test runtime. §4.1: dp-validate 10⁴ samples <10 s/mechanism. Formula: samples × per_sample ≈ 10⁴ × 1 ms = 10 s; N mechanisms over c cores (rayon, §4.3) → N/c × 10 s wall-clock.

C11 — Parallel query throughput. §4.3: ≤64 threads; each query <10 µs (C3). Formula: 64 × 10⁵ q/s = 6.4×10⁶ queries/s aggregate (compute-bound; only the per-dataset budget mutex is shared).

C12 — RDP order-vector sizing. §4.5: orders in [1.01, 256], ≥20 log-spaced. Formula: 20–100 orders × 8 B = 160 B–800 B per curve; order count trades accounting tightness (more orders → tighter (ε,δ)) against the O(|orders|) compose cost (C6).

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each requires synthesis of ≥2 sections; the named anchors are where the answer is assembled.

  1. Why is DP a property of the mechanism, not the output, and what misuse follows from treating a single noised value as "anonymized"? (MM1 + §8 + Phase 1)
  2. A developer computes sensitivity from the actual dataset, not the worst case. Trace how this voids the ε guarantee and which invariant catches it. (MM2 + Phase 2 + I6 + I10)
  3. Explain the floating-point Laplace attack and why snapping defeats it. Which ADR and invariant mandate it? (MM3 + ADR-05 + I5 + Phase 5)
  4. Why does converting RDP to (ε,δ) after every step inflate the reported ε, and when must you convert? (MM5 + ADR-08 + Phase 9 + §4.5)
  5. Show the TOCTOU race in a non-atomic budget spend and how the ledger prevents two queries from overspending. (MM6 + ADR-12 + Phase 11 + §4.3)
  6. The post-processing theorem makes debiasing randomized-response counts free. Why, and what budget bug does forgetting it cause? (MM7 + Phase 7 + Phase 13 + MM4)
  7. In DP-SGD, why must clipping be per-example and before averaging? What breaks if you clip the mean gradient? (MM8 + ADR-13 + Phase 13)
  8. Reconcile the §4.2 audit-log ceiling (<10 MB) with the 1M-entry cap at ~64 B/entry. At what entry count does rotation trigger? (C9 + §4.2 + §4.6)
  9. Compare basic, advanced, and RDP composition for 10⁴ Gaussian queries. Which gives the smallest ε and why? (MM4 + Phase 8 + Phase 9 + ADR-08)
  10. Why f64 throughout and never f32 on privacy-critical paths? Connect to snapping and analytic-Gaussian precision. (§4.5 + MM3 + ADR-07 + Phase 4)
  11. The analytic Gaussian saves 10–30% σ over the classical bound. What is the engineering cost, and when is the classical bound preferable? (ADR-07 + Phase 4 + Comparative table)
  12. Distinguish ε-DP, (ε,δ)-DP, RDP, and zCDP. Which mechanisms suit each, and why keep separate RDP and zCDP accumulators? (Phase 3 + Phase 4 + Phase 9 + Phase 10 + ADR-09)
  13. A 1000-bin histogram is budgeted at <1 ms. Why does parallel composition let all bins share one ε rather than spending 1000ε? (C2 + MM4 + Phase 8 + Phase 12)
  14. Why must the CSPRNG be OS-seeded and one-state-per-thread? What does each property prevent? (ADR-04 + §4.3 + I5)
  15. The engine declares + audits sensitivity (ADR-27/29) rather than proving it via types like OpenDP. Give two regimes where OpenDP's approach is right. (ADR-27 + ADR-29 + Comparative table)
  16. How does dp-validate empirically detect a σ wrong by 0.01%, and why can't unit tests catch it? (ADR-18 + Phase 14 + §4.5)
  17. Trace the privacy cost of a bounded-mean query that spends budget twice. Why two charges, and how does ADR-31's neighboring relation change sensitivity? (Phase 12 + ADR-31 + Phase 2)
  18. Why does subsampling improve DP-SGD privacy, where is the amplification accounted, and what is the per-step budget for batch 256 over 10⁴ steps? (MM8 + Phase 13 + Phase 10 + C7)
  19. The budget is per-dataset (ADR-30). Show how a global budget lets one analyst exhaust another's quota, and which invariant the per-dataset design enforces. (ADR-30 + Phase 11 + I3)
  20. The exponential mechanism uses log-sum-exp. What numerical failure does it prevent, and how does its sensitivity differ from additive-noise mechanisms? (Phase 6 + §4.5 + Phase 2)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Fundamentally different designs for the same goal — not parameter tweaks.

Alt-1 — Type-level sensitivity calculus (OpenDP-style)

Transformation: f with stability c  ┐
                                     ├─chain─▶ Measurement (ε,δ)   (sensitivity proven by types)
Measurement: M with privacy (ε,δ)   ┘

Transformations and measurements compose with compile-time stability tracking; sensitivity is proven, not declared. Wins in high-assurance settings that want correct-by-construction sensitivity. Real system: OpenDP (the transformation/measurement framework). Rejected here because it dramatically raises API complexity and compile time (ADR-27, ADR-29); the declared-and-audited model is more approachable and is validated empirically by dp-validate.

Alt-2 — Syntactic anonymization (k-anonymity / l-diversity)

quasi-identifiers ──generalize/suppress──▶ each record indistinguishable among k
(no noise; deterministic transform of the table)

Generalize or suppress quasi-identifiers so every record is hidden among k peers. Wins for simple, interpretable static releases where formal DP is not required and analysts want to read the released table directly. Real systems: ARX, Mondrian, Datafly. Rejected here because it provides no formal privacy guarantee and is vulnerable to composition and linkage attacks — precisely the failure mode that motivated DP (Dwork et al. 2006).

Alt-3 — Local DP only (no trusted curator)

each user ──randomize on device (RR)──▶ aggregator sees only randomized reports
                                         (curator never holds raw data)

Every user randomizes their own data before it leaves the device; the aggregator never sees raw values. Wins when no trusted curator can exist — large-scale telemetry. Real systems: Google RAPPOR, Apple's LDP telemetry. Rejected as the default because local DP needs far more users for the same accuracy; this engine offers it at Phase 13 but centers the more accurate trusted-curator model.

Alt-4 — Secure aggregation / MPC curator replacement

parties ──MPC / secure aggregation──▶ noised aggregate revealed
         (no single party sees raw data or the un-noised sum)

Parties compute the noised aggregate under MPC so no single party sees raw data or the clean sum — central-DP accuracy without a trusted curator. Wins for federated learning across mutually distrustful parties. Real systems: secure aggregation in federated learning (Bonawitz et al. 2017). Rejected here (deferred; cross-links #392 MPC and #407 Federated Learning) because it adds a cryptographic protocol layer orthogonal to the DP accounting this engine teaches.

Deferred Features Register

Every deferred item anchors to a real section/phase and names its un-defer trigger.

Feature Anchor Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
Formal verification / proof-carrying mechanism proofs Phase 14 — hardening + conformance Deferred (post-1.0) A deployment must ship machine-checked privacy proofs
Replace-one / local sensitivity (bounded-DP refinements) Phase 14 — hardening + conformance Deferred (post-1.0) Analyses need the tighter bounded-DP sensitivity with its own proof
Shuffle model of local DP (amplification) Phase 13 — local DP + DP-SGD + CLI Deferred (post-1.0) Telemetry needs shuffle-model amplification between local and central DP
Distributed budget coordination (multi-node) Phase 11 — privacy budget manager Deferred (post-1.0) The budget ledger must be shared across multiple processes/nodes
Type-level sensitivity tracking (OpenDP-style) §9 Tech Stack ADR Table (ADR-27/29) Deferred (post-1.0) A high-assurance setting justifies correct-by-construction sensitivity
Secure aggregation / MPC curator replacement §2 Why Build This (cross-links #392/#407) Deferred (post-1.0) The trusted-curator assumption must be removed

Glossary

Alphabetical. Terms defined with precision for the dp-* crates. Implementation crate noted where applicable.

Above threshold : SVT primitive. Add Lap(2/eps) to threshold T; add Lap(4/eps) to each query answer. : Release YES when noisy query exceeds noisy threshold; NO answers consume zero budget (up to max YES count c). : Each YES charges eps budget. Implemented in dp-svt/src/above_threshold.rs.

Accountant : Component that tracks cumulative privacy loss across sequential releases. : Outputs a certified (eps, delta) upper bound on demand via to_privacy_params(). : Subtypes: basic, advanced, RDP, zCDP, moments. All implement the Accountant trait in dp-accountant.

Add/remove neighboring : Two datasets D, D' are neighbors if one is obtained by adding or removing exactly one record. : Default neighboring relation in dp-sensitivity. Determines all L1/L2 sensitivity formulas. : Contrasts with replace-one neighboring, which doubles sensitivity.

Analytic Gaussian : Balle-Wang (ICML 2018) exact sigma calibration for the Gaussian mechanism. : Solves the (eps, delta) integral equation numerically via bisection; always yields smaller sigma than the classical bound. : Classical bound: sigma >= sqrt(2 ln(1.25/delta)) * l2 / eps. Analytic is strictly tighter. : Default in dp-mechanism/src/gaussian.rs.

Basic composition : k sequential mechanisms (eps_i, delta_i) compose to (sum eps_i, sum delta_i)-DP. : Simplest accountant; dominated by advanced composition for large k and small delta. : Auto-choose min(basic, advanced) in dp-accountant/src/basic.rs.

Bounded query : A query with finite global sensitivity because all inputs are clamped to [lower, upper] before aggregation. : Required for calibrating any finite noise level. Enforced by dp-sensitivity clamping. : Invariant I6: every value entering an aggregate must be within its declared Bounds.

Budget : Per-dataset (eps, delta) ceiling: Budget { total: PrivacyParams, spent: PrivacyLoss }. : Each release charges the accountant, updates spent, and is denied (DpError::BudgetExhausted) if spent would exceed total. : Atomic spend; fail-closed; per-analyst variant tracked via Ledger. Invariant I3, I10.

Budget exhaustion : State when cumulative (eps, delta) charged against a dataset reaches or would exceed the ceiling. : All subsequent queries denied. Budget cannot go negative. : Invariant I3. Enforced by dp-budget/src/policy.rs.

Central DP : Privacy model where a trusted data curator holds raw data, applies a mechanism, and releases only the noisy result. : The dp-* engine operates in central-DP mode. Contrasts with local DP (dp-local).

Clamping / clipping : Clamping: enforce lower <= x <= upper on every data value before aggregation (I6, P7). : Makes global sensitivity finite; required for bounded queries. : In dp-dpsgd: L2 norm clipping rescales per-example gradient g to min(1, C/||g||_2)*g. : Both operations bound sensitivity at the source.

Composition : Combining outputs of multiple DP mechanisms on the same dataset. : Sequential: privacy losses accumulate (basic or advanced composition, Phase 8). : Parallel: mechanisms on disjoint partitions compose at max(eps_i), not sum. : Correctness invariant I4: accountant's reported loss is an upper bound on the true composed loss.

Concentrated DP (zCDP) : Privacy parameterized by rho; the privacy loss random variable is sub-Gaussian with parameter rho. : Composes additively: rho_total = sum(rho_i). Converts to (eps, delta) at query time. : Tighter than basic composition for repeated Gaussian releases. : Implemented in dp-accountant/src/zcdp.rs. See also: rho.

CSPRNG : Cryptographically secure pseudorandom number generator; seeded from OS entropy (getrandom). : All noise in dp-sample drawn from a CSPRNG. Naive std::rand / SmallRng banned in deny.toml. : Required by secure-sampling fidelity invariant I5.

Delta : Failure probability allowing the eps-bound to be violated; newtype Delta(f64) in [0,1). : delta=0 = pure DP. Gaussian mechanism requires delta > 0 (I8). : Deltas add under basic composition; accumulate in the accountant's spent field.

Debiasing : Converts biased local-DP frequency counts (raw RR TRUE counts) to unbiased estimates. : Formula: f_hat = (count - n*(1-p)) / (2p-1); variance = p*(1-p) / (n*(2p-1)^2). : Implemented in dp-local/src/frequency.rs.

Discrete Gaussian : Integer-valued Gaussian (Canonne-Kamath-Steinke, NeurIPS 2020). : Eliminates floating-point representability gaps that the snapping mechanism addresses for Laplace. : Properties: zero-mean, variance sigma^2, closed under addition. : Implemented in dp-sample/src/gaussian.rs.

DP-SGD : Differentially private SGD. Four steps per mini-batch: : (1) Clip per-example L2 gradient norms to C. (2) Sum clipped gradients. : (3) Add Gaussian noise: sigma = C * noise_multiplier. (4) Track epsilon via moments accountant. : Implemented across dp-dpsgd/clip.rs, noise.rs, tracker.rs, trainer.rs.

Epsilon : Privacy-loss parameter; newtype Epsilon(f64) > 0 and finite (I8). : Smaller = stronger privacy. Every DpResult<T> carries an epsilon via PrivacyParams stamp. : Epsilons add under basic composition; minimized by RDP-to-(eps,delta) conversion over alpha.

Exponential mechanism : Selects candidate c with probability proportional to exp(eps * u(c) / (2 * sensitivity)). : Satisfies eps-DP. Computed via log-sum-exp for numerical stability (avoids overflow). : Used for non-numeric selection: mode, best split, quantile bucket. : Implemented in dp-mechanism/src/exponential.rs.

Fail-closed : I10 policy: any invalid parameter, exhausted budget, or unbounded sensitivity causes DpError and refusal. : No silent release with an incorrect guarantee. : Enforced at: Epsilon/Delta construction, Budget::spend, Sensitivity construction.

Float-DP attack : Mironov (CCS 2012): a naive floating-point Laplace sampler leaks the exact input value. : Cause: inversion formula x = -b*sign(u)*ln(1-2|u|) maps each input to a deterministic set of representable floats. : Fix: snapping mechanism in dp-sample/src/laplace.rs. Invariant I5.

Frequency oracle : Aggregation of local-DP randomized responses into an unbiased domain-wide frequency estimate. : Debiasing formula applied per domain value. Variance scales O(1/(n*(2p-1)^2)). : Implemented in dp-local/src/frequency.rs.

Global sensitivity : sup_{D, D' neighboring} |f(D) - f(D')|, in L1 or L2 norm. : Laplace uses L1; Gaussian uses L2. Declared sensitivity must satisfy I2: declared >= true. : Computed by dp-sensitivity/src/global.rs for count, sum, mean.

Gradient clipping : Per-example: g_clipped = g * min(1, C/||g||_2). Bounds L2 sensitivity of the batch sum at C. : Invariant: ||g_clipped||_2 <= C for every example. : Enables noise calibration: sigma = C * noise_multiplier. Implemented in dp-dpsgd/src/clip.rs.

Heavy hitter : A domain value appearing in a large fraction of local-DP reports. : Estimated via domain reduction (hashing) + multi-channel RR in dp-local/src/heavyhitter.rs. : Privacy cost equals a single RR per hash level.

Histogram query : Parallel composition: each bin is a count query over a disjoint data partition. : Budget cost = max(eps_i) over bins, not sum. : Sensitivity per bin = 1. Implemented in dp-query/src/histogram.rs.

L1 sensitivity : sup_{D,D'} |f(D)-f(D')|_1. Used to calibrate Laplace scale b = l1/eps. : Component of Sensitivity { l1, l2 }. count: l1=1; sum over [lo,hi]: l1=hi-lo.

L2 sensitivity : sup_{D,D'} |f(D)-f(D')|_2. Used to calibrate Gaussian sigma. : Component of Sensitivity { l1, l2 }. sum over [lo,hi]: l2=hi-lo.

Laplace mechanism : Adds Lap(b) noise where b = l1/eps; pure eps-DP. : Requires snapping sampler to close Mironov float-DP attack. : Advertises PrivacyParams { epsilon: eps, delta: Delta(0.0) }. : Implemented in dp-mechanism/src/laplace.rs.

Local DP : Each user perturbs their own data before sending; no trusted curator. : Stronger per-record guarantee, weaker population accuracy. : Epsilon is per-user per-record. Implemented in dp-local.

Local sensitivity : sup_{D' neighboring D} |f(D)-f(D')|; data-dependent. : Cannot be used directly without smooth sensitivity amplification. : Not exposed without explicit wrapping; dp-sensitivity defaults to global sensitivity.

Log-sum-exp trick : Numerically stable: ln(sum_i exp(a_i)) = a_max + ln(sum_i exp(a_i - a_max)). : Required in exponential mechanism to avoid overflow for large utility ranges (>700 units). : Implemented in dp-mechanism/src/exponential.rs.

Mechanism trait : fn privatize(&self, exact: T, sens: Sensitivity) -> T : fn privacy_params(&self) -> PrivacyParams : Every mechanism must implement both. Defined in dp-mechanism/src/lib.rs.

Moments accountant : Specialization of RDP for the subsampled Gaussian mechanism. : Tracks log E[e^{(alpha-1)*L}] where L is the privacy loss variable. : Enables tight per-step epsilon for DP-SGD (Abadi et al., CCS 2016). : Subsampling amplification provably included. Implemented in dp-accountant/src/moments.rs.

Neighboring datasets : Two datasets D, D' differing by one record under the chosen neighboring relation. : The definition determines sensitivity of every query. : dp-sensitivity accepts the relation as a parameter (add/remove or replace-one).

Noise calibration : Setting noise scale as a function of sensitivity and (eps, delta) so the mechanism satisfies its DP claim exactly. : Laplace: b = l1/eps (exact). Gaussian: analytic Balle-Wang (exact). Classical Gaussian: upper bound.

Noise multiplier : In DP-SGD: sigma = C * noise_multiplier, where C is the gradient clipping bound. : Moments accountant maps (noise_multiplier, T steps, q sample rate) to final epsilon at target delta. : Increasing noise_multiplier improves privacy, reduces utility.

Parallel composition : Disjoint dataset partitions processed independently compose at max(eps_i), not sum. : Enables histograms and per-stratum queries without multiplicative budget cost. : Formalized alongside sequential composition in dp-accountant/src/basic.rs.

Post-processing : Any function of a DP output is DP with the same (eps, delta) parameters (I7, P5). : DpResult<T> can be transformed freely — no re-noising required. : DP is defined over output distributions, not computation graphs.

Privacy amplification : Running a mechanism on a random subsample of m < N records amplifies privacy. : Effective eps_sub << eps for small subsampling rate q = m/N. : Quantified only through moments/RDP accountant — never assumed (P9).

Privacy budget : Total (eps, delta) allocated to a dataset or analyst, held in Budget. : Each mechanism release charges the accountant and updates spent. : Query that would push spent past total is denied (I3, I10). Audit log persists every charge.

Privacy loss : Random variable L(D,D') = ln(Pr[M(D) in S] / Pr[M(D') in S]). : Must satisfy L <= eps with probability >= 1-delta for (eps,delta)-DP. : Tracked as a distribution (RDP) or scalar (basic composition) by the accountant.

Privacy stamp : DpResult<T> carries both the released value and PrivacyParams { epsilon, delta }. : Post-processing-immune (I7): downstream transforms do not invalidate the stamp. : Enables downstream systems to verify the DP guarantee without re-computation.

Pure DP : (eps, 0)-DP: no delta failure probability. Hardest guarantee. : Achieved by Laplace mechanism and randomized response. : Gaussian mechanism cannot achieve pure DP. Implies (eps, delta)-DP for any delta > 0.

Randomized response : Local-DP primitive: return true answer with probability p, flip with probability 1-p. : Satisfies eps = ln(p/(1-p))-DP. Debiasing formula recovers unbiased frequencies. : Implemented in dp-local/src/rr.rs.

Renyi DP (RDP) : Parameterized by order alpha; tracks alpha-Renyi divergence D_alpha(M(D)||M(D')). : Composes by addition per order. Converts to tightest (eps,delta) via minimization over alpha. : Gaussian mechanism: RDP at order alpha = alpha/(2 sigma^2). (Mironov, CSF 2017). : Implemented in dp-accountant/src/rdp.rs.

Replace-one neighboring : Two datasets differ by replacing exactly one record with an arbitrary record. : Doubles sensitivity relative to add/remove. : dp-sensitivity exposes the relation as a parameter so callers choose the model.

Report noisy max : Add independent Lap(1/eps) noise to each candidate count; return the argmax. : Satisfies eps-DP for the selection. Cheaper than exponential when candidates are numeric counts. : Differs from exponential in additive noise vs softmax weighting. : Implemented in dp-mechanism/src/rnm.rs.

Rho (zCDP parameter) : Sub-Gaussianity parameter of zero-concentrated DP. : Gaussian mechanism: rho = l2^2 / (2 sigma^2). Composes additively. : Converts to (eps,delta): eps = rho + 2*sqrt(rho * ln(1/delta)). : Tracked in ZcdpAccountant.

Secure sampling : Noise generation that resists the Mironov float-DP attack. : Requirements: snapping mechanism for Laplace, discrete Gaussian for integers, CSPRNG-only seeding. : All of dp-sample embodies P3. I5 formalizes the correctness requirement.

Sensitivity soundness : I2: declared sensitivity >= true global sensitivity for the clamped query. : Over-estimation: safe (more noise, less utility, but correct). : Under-estimation: silently violates DP — the worst failure mode. : Enforced by clamping in dp-sensitivity; unbounded input fails with DpError::UnboundedSensitivity.

Snapping mechanism : Mironov (CCS 2012): rounds a Laplace sample to the nearest float on a lattice scaled to machine precision. : Provably closes the representable-value leakage channel. : Key property: the set of possible outputs is independent of the exact input value. : Mandatory in dp-sample/src/laplace.rs.

Sparse vector technique (SVT) : Answers a long sequence of threshold queries cheaply. : Budget paid only for YES answers (above threshold); NO answers free up to count cap c. : Useful for streaming queries where most answers expected to be NO. : Implemented in dp-svt/src/svt.rs and dp-svt/src/above_threshold.rs.

Stability (transform) : A transform T has stability t if neighboring inputs produce at most t-neighboring outputs. : Sensitivity scales by t through the transform: new_sensitivity = old_sensitivity * t. : Propagated in dp-sensitivity/src/stability.rs; ensures I2 across multi-step pipelines.

StatDP : Ding et al. (CCS 2018) methodology for empirical DP verification. : Draw n samples from M(D) and M(D') for neighboring D, D'; run likelihood-ratio test for (eps,delta) compliance. : Basis of dp-validate/src/hypothesis.rs.

Subsampling : Drawing m < N records before applying a mechanism amplifies privacy. : Only valid when accounted through moments/RDP accountant (P9). : Never assumed; always proved. Amplification factor decreases as q = m/N increases.

Utility function : In the exponential mechanism: u: Candidate -> f64 scoring function. : Higher score = exponentially more likely to be selected. : Sensitivity = max_{D,D' neighboring} max_c |u_D(c) - u_D'(c)|.

Zero-concentrated DP (zCDP) : See Concentrated DP and Rho. : Intermediate representation between RDP and (eps,delta)-DP. : Compositionally convenient: rho values add; conversion to (eps,delta) done at release time. : Implemented in dp-accountant/src/zcdp.rs.

dp-cli/src/cmd_mechanism.rs mechanism subcommand: run named mechanism on stdin with given params; print stamp.
dp-cli/src/cmd_validate.rs validate subcommand: run statistical conformance tests; print pass/fail and statistics.
dp-cli/tests/cli.rs Integration tests via assert_cmd; covers all four subcommands end-to-end.
benches/laplace_throughput.rs Criterion: Laplace mechanism throughput vs dataset size (1k–1M rows).
benches/accountant_scale.rs Criterion: RDP accountant cost for 1000 DP-SGD steps; conversion latency.
benches/budget_contention.rs Criterion: concurrent budget spend under thread contention; atomic overhead.
examples/census_count.rs End-to-end: DP count on synthetic census-like data; prints DpResult JSON.
examples/dpsgd_mnist.rs End-to-end: DP-SGD on synthetic MNIST-like gradient data; prints per-epoch epsilon.
examples/svt_stream.rs SVT on stream of range queries with fixed threshold; prints YES/NO and final budget.

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Total timeline: 28 weeks across Phases 1–14. Phases 1–7 build the mechanism and sampling stack. Phases 8–11 build the accounting and budget layer. Phases 12–14 integrate, harden, and validate. Durations weight numerically and statistically intensive phases longer. The final row is the integration checkpoint.

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
Phase 1 — Core Types dp-core: Epsilon, Delta, PrivacyParams, Sensitivity, Bounds, DpResult, DpError newtypes; invariant enforcement; arithmetic 1 week Newtype arithmetic allows epsilon/scale transposition; proptest coverage too shallow for edge cases All invariant property tests pass; eps=0, delta<0, lower>=upper, NaN/Inf all return DpError; valid arithmetic verified by proptest
Phase 2 — Sensitivity Analysis dp-sensitivity: L1/L2 sensitivity for count/sum/mean; clamping to Bounds; transform stability propagation; soundness rule 1.5 weeks Sensitivity under-estimation silently violates DP (I2); add/remove vs replace-one confusion doubles error; stability not propagated through multi-step pipelines Sensitivity tests for all query types under both neighboring relations; soundness property: declared >= analytic; I6 clamping tested; unbounded input rejected with DpError::UnboundedSensitivity
Phase 3 — Laplace Mechanism dp-mechanism Laplace: b=l1/eps calibration; PrivacyParams advertisement; wired to dp-sample (stub CSPRNG pending Phase 5) 1 week Naive float sampler prematurely used before snapping (float-DP attack window); scale formula inverted (eps/l1 instead of l1/eps) Calibration unit tests; pure eps-DP params advertised; sampler stub annotated for Phase 5 replacement
Phase 4 — Gaussian Mechanism dp-mechanism Gaussian: analytic Balle-Wang sigma; delta>0 enforcement; classical vs analytic comparison table 1.5 weeks Classical sigma wastes utility by up to 2x; delta=0 not rejected causes infinite noise; wrong equation branch selected at boundary Analytic sigma matches Balle-Wang reference values to 1e-9; delta=0 rejected (I8); (eps,delta) params correct; comparison table shows analytic strictly tighter
Phase 5 — Secure Sampling dp-sample: snapping Laplace; discrete/interval-refinement Gaussian; CSPRNG from OS entropy; no low-bit gaps 2 weeks Float-DP attack open if Phase 3 stub not replaced; weak CSPRNG seed; discrete Gaussian off-by-one at tails; SmallRng slips through KS goodness-of-fit (n=100k, p>0.05); float-gap regression confirms snapping closes attack; OS-entropy seeding verified; SmallRng banned in deny.toml
Phase 6 — Exponential Mechanism dp-mechanism exponential: log-sum-exp stable selection; generic candidate type; eps-DP 1 week Naive exp sum overflows for utility range >700 units; candidate distribution biased without log-sum-exp stabilization Overflow test for utilities spanning 1000 units; chi-squared distribution test; eps-DP params correct for multiple utility functions
Phase 7 — Report Noisy Max + RR Report-noisy-max (Laplace-noised argmax) and randomized response (flip probability p, debiasing estimator) 1 week RNM epsilon miscounted as eps/2; RR debiasing denominator (2p-1) off by a factor; local vs central eps calibration confused RNM vs exponential equivalence on uniform utilities; RR debiasing round-trip within 5% at n=10k; eps=ln(p/(1-p)) verified algebraically
Phase 8 — Basic + Advanced Composition dp-accountant: basic (sum eps/delta); advanced (sqrt-k scaling); sequential vs parallel; heterogeneous mechanism types 1.5 weeks Advanced composition applied when basic is tighter; parallel composition used on non-disjoint data; delta double-counted Composition cross-checks basic vs advanced; monotonicity (I9); parallel composition max not sum on disjoint test; heterogeneous mechanism test
Phase 9 — Renyi DP dp-accountant RDP: alpha-order grid; per-mechanism contributions; RDP-to-(eps,delta) conversion via minimization 2 weeks Sparse alpha grid misses tightest (eps,delta); numerical precision loss in conversion; RDP not shown tighter than advanced for Gaussian RDP sum property; conversion matches Mironov (2017) reference values to 1e-6; comparison table: RDP < advanced for repeated Gaussian releases
Phase 10 — zCDP + Moments Accountant zCDP rho accumulator; moments accountant for subsampled Gaussian; subsampling amplification formula 2 weeks Subsampling amplification assumed not proved (violates P9); rho conversion formula error; moments accountant diverges from Abadi reference rho additivity property; moments accountant matches Abadi et al. (2016) reference epsilons; subsampling amplification provably included in tracker
Phase 11 — Privacy Budget Manager dp-budget: per-dataset/analyst ledger; atomic spend; hard ceiling; append-only audit log; fail-closed denial 1.5 weeks Race condition under concurrent spend allows over-draw; decrement before ceiling check; audit log entries lost on error path Concurrent spend test (100 threads): no over-draw; negative balance impossible; denial fires at exact ceiling; audit log entries verified for every charge
Phase 12 — DP Query Interface dp-query: count, sum, mean, histogram (parallel composition), quantile (exponential); sensitivity wiring; budget charging 2 weeks Mean charged once but two sub-operations execute; histogram uses sequential instead of parallel; quantile sensitivity wrong for non-rank utilities Integration tests: DpResult stamp correct; budget charged exactly once per query; histogram parallel composition cost = max not sum; quantile distribution test
Phase 13 — Local DP + DP-SGD + CLI dp-local RR + debiasing + heavy-hitter; dp-dpsgd clip + noise + moments tracker; dp-cli all four subcommands 3 weeks Clipping bound C not wired to Gaussian sigma calibration; CLI bypasses budget checks on fast path; RR frequency estimator variance not verified DP-SGD epsilon monotone over training steps; per-example gradient norms <= C post-clip; all CLI assert_cmd tests pass; frequency estimator within 2 sigma of true at n=10k
Phase 14 — Hardening + Validation + Perf dp-validate StatDP-style tests + sensitivity audits + accountant cross-checks + fuzz; benchmarks recorded; final packing 2.5 weeks Statistical tests underpowered for subtle miscalibration; fuzz triggers panic on NaN inputs; sampler bias not detected at small n; benchmark regressions not baselined All neighboring-dataset tests pass at n=500k; no panics on 1M fuzz inputs; sensitivity audit matches declared values; benchmark baselines committed; CHANGELOG updated for all phases
Full Build Complete All 14 phases integrated; CI green; cargo-deny clean; examples produce valid DpResult JSON; invariants I1–I10 verified by test mapping — Late accountant divergence (RDP vs advanced for non-Gaussian mechanisms) requires Phase 9 rework; stat tests need large sample budget in CI time budget CI passes all suites including statistical conformance; cargo-deny clean; examples runnable end-to-end; I1–I10 test matrix documented and linked in README
